# Conjecture K: the ledger

*Every statement about Conjecture K in its current form, with its scope, its status and where it is proved and
checked. The book [`MONOGRAPH.md`](MONOGRAPH.md) tells the same state as a text; this ledger as it stood with its
dated passages is [`sources/status-2026-11-15/STATUS_with_history.md`](sources/status-2026-11-15/STATUS_with_history.md).*

Conjecture K is **open**. Its statement and notation are in [`MONOGRAPH.md`](MONOGRAPH.md), chapter 1, and in
[`README.md`](README.md), part (a). This file is edited in place: a row always states the strongest form that is
known, with the evidence for that form.

## 0. How to read this file

**Labels.**

| label | meaning |
|---|---|
| **P** | proved on paper, and the proof was re-derived by someone other than its author; the status names both. **P, author only**: re-derived only within the author's own session |
| **C** | certified by an exact or interval checker that someone other than its author replayed, with the implication from the check to the statement inspected. **C, author only**: replayed only within the author's session. A replay runs the delivered code; "interval code not audited" means that nobody read that code line by line |
| **Lean** | a theorem of the Lean development of this repository (the row names it) |
| **N** | numerical only (floating point), with its stated accuracy. A search gives candidate values and lower bounds on a supremum, never an upper bound |
| **R** | read from the record without full independent verification. This includes certificates whose delivered checker was only run, by its recipient or by the user, with no inspection of the implication from the check to the statement |
| **open** | not proved; no claim |
| **false** | shown false; section 13 gives the counterexample and its status |

A status is never higher than its source supports. Where sources disagree, the later independent check stands, and
the row states only what stands.

**Who checked what.** codex-a713 wrote the independent audit REP (with CERT, ASM, ADD, KER) and a separate second
audit PF (with REC, DIF, OPT); "audit" in a status means REP and its companions, "second audit" PF and its
supplements. codex-a7c4 wrote A15, A15R, A15T, A15F, A15V and A15S. claude-b9ee reviewed A15 and A15R (the files
`REVIEW_claude-b9ee.md` in their folders), A15T (rev. #403, archived in A15F), A15F (rev. #409, archived in A15V),
A15V (rev. #413, archived in A15S) and A15S (rev. #419, the review of that pull request),
the external report EXT-P (REV) and the report TILT of codex-6e2b (TILT-REV to TILT-REV4; in TILT-REV4 a reviewer
agent of the same session made most re-derivations, as that review says). claude-b9ee is the recipient of nearly
every delivery of the sources table; where a row gives **C** with claude-b9ee as checker, the cited review says
exactly what was read, re-derived and replayed.

**Row IDs** are stable labels, not a sequence: other documents cite them, and gaps are normal. A row that states
what several labels stated carries all of them. The item numbers of sections 13 and 14 (1 to 107, with gaps) are
labels of the same kind.

**Labels without a row.** Their former text is in `sources/status-2026-11-15/STATUS_with_history.md`.

**Rows of the profile box.** "The three rows" means:

| row | ε | ℓ | β = b | a = ℓ − 2β | σ\* (maximiser of Λ(σW)) |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| peak | 1891/2500 = 0.7564 | 1057/1250 = 0.8456 | 179/1250 = 0.1432 | 699/1250 = 0.5592 | 0.2009 |
| flank | 149/200 = 0.745 | 4267/5000 = 0.8534 | 1467/10000 = 0.1467 | 14/25 = 0.56 | 0.1726 |
| upper | 1891/2500 = 0.7564 | 0.877 | 19/125 = 0.152 | 573/1000 = 0.573 | 0.1617 |

Peak constants: p = 1/ε = 2500/1891; B₀ = Λ(0) = 2E\|L\| = 387√π/500 ∈ (1.371879280600869, 1.371879280600870);
1.3739535256614 < S < 1.3739535256616 (3.14), attained near v₀ = σ\*² = 403448/10⁷; S − B₀ > 0.002074245;
j₀ = ½E\|L\|⁻¹ ∈ [1.431973791291431, 1.431973791291432]; g₀ = g(0) ∈ [−0.835135932614130, −0.835135932614129];
k_A = p − E[(T − 1)²/(2\|L\|)] = 0.320054504.

**Notation of the rows.** T = \|z₁\|², L = z₁(a + bT), F(G) = E\|L + G\| + E\|L − G\| for every G (so Λ(G) = F(G) −
‖G‖²/ε; F(G) = 2E\|L + G\| when G is even in z₁, in particular F(q) = 2E\|L + q\| for a remote q), B(u) = 2E\|L + √u·W\|, D(q) = B(u) − F(q)
(the deficit), D₊ = max(D, 0), u = ‖q‖², δ = E\|q\|⁴ − 2u², χ = δ/u², w = ‖r‖² for the A-shell form r. Profiles:
E_{z₁}ph(L + y) = y·j(\|y\|²), E_{z₁}[(T − 1)ph(L + y)] = y·g(\|y\|²); C(q) = ‖Π₁,₀[q·g(\|q\|²)]‖ (the A-content);
h_s = (g − g₀) − s(j₀ − j) and c_s(q) = Π₁[q·h_s(\|q\|²)] (the tilted content). In section 11, A = T − 2/5,
P = p − 9/25 = 0.962052, 𝒥(q, r) = 2E\|L + q + A·r\| − P‖r‖² − F(q) and k = P − 13/20 = 59009/189100.

## 1. Summary

1. The exact gate-free row (2.1) is proved. Conjecture K (2.2) is open, and no violation of it is known (2.3, **N**).
2. **Remote part (L1).** The supremum over remote cubics of all energies and finite dimensions equals the corresponding Gaussian benchmark at the peak (3.3a) and at the named flank and upper profiles (3.15a, both **P + C, author only**). At the peak 3.3a also gives strict same-variance domination for 0 < u ≤ 0.0463. The independently replayed absolute cap (3.3), narrow tube (3.11) and one-sided rectangle (3.15) give quantitative bounds near the peak. Over unrestricted profiles and prices the exact remote and sharp-radius questions (3.5–3.6) remain open. The comparison holds in every dimension for 0 < χ ≤ 1/72000 (1/73500 via phase averaging, 3.18, **P + C, author only**). It also holds for cubics that vanish on a complex hyperplane and for those with P₃,₁(q²) = 0 (3.25, 3.24, both **P, author only**), as well as for arbitrary multi-driver $(2,1)$ Wick sums $\sum_{j=1}^m c_j X_j(\lvert X_j\rvert^2 - 2)/\sqrt{2}$ ($m \ge 1$) plus any circular Gaussian scale mixture $Q_{\mathrm{mix}}$ independent of $X$ (with visible $z_1$ independent of $(X, Q_{\mathrm{mix}})$) across all profiles $a > 0, b \ge 0$ and prices $p > 0$ (3.5, 3.6, 3.25, **P + C, author only**, KWPVD; with step-by-step CLT and equal-weight doubling monotonicity proved for every $m \ge 1$ and diffuse unequal-weight doubling for $\max_j u_j \le u/7$ in 3.20). The earlier peak deficit estimates cover the small-excess and small-energy regimes used by 3.3a (3.19, 3.29, **P + C, author only**).
3. **Disjoint drivers (L2).** Λ(q + (T − 1)U) ≤ Λ(q) − (1/ε − 197/(200ℓ))‖U‖² on the whole box at every amplitude
   when z₁, q, U are independent and U is a centred proper Gaussian (4.2, **P + C**).
4. **Shared drivers, explicit regions (L3).** At the peak, orthogonality alone gives Λ < S + 10⁻⁴ for
   u ≤ 0.00558 + 0.31907w, and for u ≤ 0.0463 and w ≥ 0.0206 (6.1.1, 6.1.1a, **P + C**); the fourth-moment cutoff w ≥ 0.01125 is
   **R** (6.1.2). For a remote Gaussian linear form U with w = ‖U‖² ≤ 0.0113 and u ≤ 0.0463, the staircase and small-influence class (χ ≤ 0.2809 across the band, all χ on u ≤ 0.00815, and τ_max(q) ≤ 0.054; 6.3.40, **P + C**) and the stdlib near-Gaussian cover χ ≤ 1/4 closing 6.1.5 (6.3.43, **P + C, author only**) give Λ(q + (T − 1)U) < S + 10⁻⁴.
5. **The A-sector of K (L4).** The cost (6.2.1) and the content inequality for structured families are
   proved (6.3.2–6.3.4, 6.3.9–6.3.9e; the complete one-driver self-interaction family across all Wick shares, arbitrary complex phases and non-Hermitian quadratics on 0.00551 ≤ u ≤ 0.0463 at the three rows in 6.3.5–6.3.7 and 6.3.13–6.3.15, **P + C, author only**; its extension by X̄H(Y) at arbitrary strength in 6.3.16, **P**, from those same envelopes; and multi-driver self-interactions Σ_{j=1}^m c_j X_j(\|X_j\|² − 2) on 0 < u ≤ 0.0463 with constant 9/100 < 1/10, together with shared-bath cofactors up to e ≤ (1/5)(Σ u_j²)/u₀ and opposite-parity even cubics up to D(q₀)/5, in 6.3.10 and 6.3.17, **P + C, author only**). For a general cubic on 0.00551 ≤ u ≤ 0.0463, high excess χ ≥ 40.32 satisfies exact C² ≤ (6/25)D(q) (6.3.41, **P + C**), independent blocks obey 6.3.42 (**P + C**), and bounded-kurtosis regimes satisfy signed C² ≤ (6/25)D(q) + 10⁻⁶ on χ ≤ 841/10000 and exact zero-slack C² ≤ (6/25)D(q) on [0, 0.0463] × [0, 49/625], on [0, 1/1600] × [0, 42] and on [0, 3/250] × [0, 1/4] — closing the near-Gaussian sliver and leaving intermediate kurtosis open for the zero-slack form (6.3.43, **P + C, author only**; 6.3.18); numerically
   the largest C²/D found is 0.0876, at u = 0.0463 (section 9).
6. **The A-sector of the retained functional Λ̂.** Closed at the peak row: for every remote cubic q with u ≤ 0.0463
   and every remote linear form r, in every dimension and at every amplitude, Λ̂(q + (T − 1)r) < S + 2·10⁻⁵ (11.51,
   from 11.39, 11.40, 11.49, 11.50 and 3.3); below S if u ≤ 0.021 (11.47); below S + 9.8·10⁻⁵ on a scaled profile
   rectangle around the peak (11.53). The benchmark S is common to Λ and Λ̂ (2.14). A sharper content coefficient
   1/5 is certified on part of its domain only (11.55, author only); 11.51 does not need it.
7. **Sector assembly for K (L5).** The B-shell cost (7.1.1) and the peak alignment of the B- and A-contents (7.1.4,
   sharpened in 7.1.6) are proved. **Global raw joint A/B closure on all of $\mathbb{C}^3$ at the three rows** — proving both the signed and robust forms of 7.4 / 7.4.5 with reserve $1/100 \gg 8.07\cdot 10^{-5}$ and the global common diagonal costs $\kappa_{\mathrm{diag}} = 7/25, 3/10, 31/100$ with no shell-energy cap (for arbitrary remote $L^2$ variables in the remainder/cost bounds, and for arbitrary-amplitude first-chaos drivers in 7.4.2, raising the sufficient strong A-content constant for diagonal A+B closure at the peak from 0.15 to $11/50 = 0.22$) — is now certified in 7.4.17 (**P + C**). At the peak, odd sectors over every remote core lower Λ by at least 1988/47275 per unit
   odd energy, with arbitrary sharing (7.2.10); odd sectors over cores with A/B shells remain open for raw K (7.2.4).
8. **Sector assembly for Λ̂ (L5).** At the peak the sharp retained supremum on $u\le0.0463$, $u+V\le0.080051$ equals $S$ across all six sectors (7.5.16); the infimum of the gap in $m$ remote coordinates is asymptotic to a positive constant divided by $m$. The first-order retained B-content is proved (7.1.7), and the joint A/B bound in its
   retained form (P) holds on all of ℂ³ (7.4.16, **P + C**), giving Λ̂(q + A + B) < S + 4936/10⁸ (**P + C**) and `< S + 29.36·10⁻⁶` (`D_+` 11.61) / `< S + 27.00·10⁻⁶` (signed 11.61, strictly `< S` on $u \in [0, 0.0354] \cup [0.0456, 0.0463]$; **P + C**, `KAST`). At the peak an explicit trial gives an all-sector bound
   below S + 10⁻⁴ on an energy region around the zero core, and below B₀ < S for every G with q = 0, with no premise
   (7.5.1); on the capped domain (u ≤ 0.0463, u + V ≤ 0.080051) it is below S + 99·10⁻⁶ (**P + C**) and `< S + 79.71·10⁻⁶` (**P + C**, `KAST`) if the averaged odd
   premise (P_Ψ) holds (7.5.3). Independently reviewed pieces cover cores without A/B shells (7.2.10), every admissible core with W ≤ 1/1800000 and wider harmonic-dependent strips (7.5.4c), and the regions 7.5.7, 7.5.8, 7.5.10, 7.5.11, and 7.5.12 (**P + C**, shift exterior \|q\| ≥ 11/8, odd-dominance cone W ≥ 179V, and unconditional E F_joint(c\*) < 50·10⁻⁶ for all V ≤ 11/100000 without assuming (P)). **Global pointwise closure `F_joint(c*) <= 0` on all of ℂ⁶ at the peak row** is certified in 7.5.13 (**P + C**), giving unconditional six-sector Λ̂(G) < S + 29.36·10⁻⁶ (`D_+` 11.61) and `< S + 27.00·10⁻⁶` (signed 11.61, strictly `< S` on $u \in [0, 0.0354] \cup [0.0456, 0.0463]$; `KAST`, from `4936/10⁸` in `KGAS`) across all of u ≤ 0.0463, u + V ≤ 0.080051, arbitrary odd energy W ≥ 0, and arbitrary remote sharing.
9. **Consumer (L6).** At the peak row an all-sector bound Λ ≤ S + η with η ≤ 10⁻⁴ covers t ∈ [0.84, 0.851] for 1.402
   (8.1, **P + C**), and 6 subdivided peak-row $t$-slabs use 7.5.13 to widen this to **`[0.8400, 0.8661]`**, closing the upper shoulder gap `[0.851, 0.863]` of 8.7 at ε = 1891/2500 (**P + C**, `KAST`); the other response regions, the other rows and a Lean consumer are open. At the fixed witness
   ε = 149/200, one new row would give 1.40171 from that witness's 19 rows (8.5; in Lean until #525, and below the
   lower bound proved since), but the tested transfer of the peak
   results to it exceeds the allowance (8.6). At ε = 299/400 the 19 rows of ε = 149/200 (in Lean until #525), moved to that witness by keeping
   the h-square, prove the cap 713263/10⁶ < 500/701 for every response with t outside (0.8165, 0.866) (8.9,
   **P + C**); ten explicit all-sector inequalities at central rows, with allowance 1/40000, would complete 1.402
   (8.10, open; `[0.84000, 0.84948]` is covered via the scaled peak row using 7.5.13 in `KAST`). Transferring the 136-node spectral mass row by exact homothety (c(ε) = (2141/3150)ε) removes the
   t ≈ 0 barrier and, with adjacent two-row mixing (`RowHolds.mix`), closes t outside (0.795, 0.864) at ε = 3/4 from
   Lean's `Lower.ρ` alone and t outside (0.7515, 0.863) at ε = 94/125 using ρ = 0.93462 (8.12, **P + C, author only**);
   thirteen central rows at ε = 94/125 then admit > 10⁻⁴ on every row (minimum > 1.021·10⁻⁴; using 7.5.13, Row 8 `[0.840, 0.847]` closes directly at `< 6.35·10⁻⁵` / `< 6.11·10⁻⁵ < 10⁻⁴` and subdivided scaled-peak slabs close `[0.8400, 0.8547]` across Rows 8–10 at ε = 94/125 and `[0.84000, 0.85168]` across Rows 4–6 at ε = 3/4 in `KAST`; 8.13 benchmarks **P + C, author only**; `KAST` transfer and scaled-peak slabs **P + C**, remaining rows open).
10. **What K would give.** K_η with η ≤ 10⁻⁴ gives about 1.402; exact K about 1.4022; no certificate with the cubic
   witness exceeds 1.4022581233 (12.2). The cubic witness without K gives 1.401700322371 (12.1); the Lean lower bound K_G^ℂ > 1.402 (`main_bounds`) comes from the identity-tail witness (`research/identity-tail/`).

By duality, sup_q E\|q\|/‖q‖₂ = sup_φ ‖P₂,₁φ‖; OPT(P₂,₁) = sup_f ‖P₂,₁f‖² by Cauchy–Schwarz, with equality at f = g; and SDP(P₂,₁) = 1. The real analogue for every single Hermite projection Π_d, namely E\|F\| ≤ √(2/π)‖F‖₂ on the real d-th chaos, is asserted without proof in Heilman–Jones–Malavolta, arXiv:2610.10477, Example 3.1 ("it can be shown"). The question is older: Janson (*Gaussian Hilbert Spaces*, 1997, Remark 7.37) asks which elements of the d-th chaos maximise ‖X‖_p/‖X‖₂, and proves that the Wick power does for even p; Larsson-Cohn (Ark. Mat. 40 (2002) 133–144) shows that for p < 2 and large d it does not, because sums of independent copies, which tend to a Gaussian, have a larger ratio. | 3.8, 12.4 | D₁ in degree 2: E\|X\| ≤ √(2/π)‖X‖₂ for X = Σλ_i(g_i² − 1), strict for X ≠ 0; also for weighted sums of independent centred Gamma variables plus a Gaussian. (a) It settles the case p = 1 of a conjecture of Brazitikos, Tang and Tkocz ("Moments of sums of exponentials, beyond CHS", arXiv:2602.03058, Remark 4): for −1 < p < 2, sup{‖Σⱼxⱼ(Eⱼ − 1)‖ₚ : n ≥ 1, x ∈ ℝⁿ, Σⱼxⱼ² = 1} equals ‖E − 1‖ₚ for −1 < p ≤ p₀ and ‖G‖ₚ for p₀ ≤ p < 2, with p₀ = −0.565…, Eⱼ i.i.d. standard exponential and G standard normal. At p = 1 the conjectured value is √(2/π): Σⱼxⱼ(Eⱼ − 1) = Σⱼ(xⱼ/2)(gⱼ² − 1 + g′ⱼ² − 1) lies in the second chaos (and is a Gamma sum), so D₁ gives ≤, and equal weights with n → ∞ give equality. The other p are open there; their §4.1 notes that their Theorem 1 (p ≥ 2) extends to Gamma sums. (b) A second, independent proof of the statement on the second chaos (normalised: F = Σλⱼh₂(gⱼ), h₂(x) = (x² − 1)/√2, Σλⱼ² = 1): by hand when max\|λⱼ\| ≤ 1/8, with E\|F\| ≤ √(2/π) − 0.0177·Σλⱼ⁴; computer-assisted when max\|λⱼ\| ∈ [1/8, 1], by a lower bound on the Fourier integral of √(2/π) − E\|F\| from envelopes of the characteristic function, positive on each of 5,053 cells in (max\|λⱼ\|, Σ_{j≥2}λⱼ⁴); IEEE double with a slack of 10⁻⁹, not interval arithmetic | real second chaos (uses Yamazato's unimodality theorem); (a) the case p = 1 of the conjecture only; (b) the second chaos, not the Gamma sums. With a first-chaos part it is proved when the second-chaos part has a spectrum symmetric about 0, and open in general (3.21 (e5)); the real degree-3 case is proved on the classes of 3.21 (e) only, and the complex charge-one case is open | D₁ **R** (written proof by a worker of claude-b9ee; not audited). (a) the identification **P** (claude-b9ee read Remark 4 in the arXiv PDF on 17 Nov 2026). (b) **P + C**, author only (an outside session of the user, 17 Nov 2026; the certificate in double precision, not interval arithmetic; claude-b9ee re-ran it and read the script, with the same conclusion, but did not re-derive Theorems 4.2 and 4.7: CERTIFIED, 5,053 cells (the note says 5,059: its adaptive first phase depends on library versions, and every final cell is checked by the rigorous bound), smallest lower bound 8.55·10⁻⁸, no failures; at 40 random spectra the bound lies below the true integral, smallest difference 0.0182) | `sources/d1/`; (a) arXiv:2602.03058, Remark 4 and §4.1; (b) TCH3a §4.2 (Theorem 4.2) and §4.3″ (Theorem 4.7), `code/third-chaos-2026-11-17/hjm-note/` |

| 3.20 | **Doubling monotonicity, a route to 3.6 (conjecture in general; proved on equal-weight and diffuse multi-driver Wick sums).** For X ∈ H₂,₁ and an independent copy X′: is E\|(X + X′)/√2\| ≥ E\|X\|? If so on all of H₂,₁, iterating and the central limit theorem give 3.6. **Proved families (`KWPVD`):** (i) *Step-by-step equal-weight CLT and doubling monotonicity:* for $q_m = m^{-1/2}\sum_{j=1}^m H(X_j)$ ($H(X) = X(\lvert X\rvert^2 - 2)/\sqrt{2}$), **$\mathbb{E}\lvert q_{m+1}\rvert > \mathbb{E}\lvert q_m\rvert$** and **$\mathbb{E}\lvert q_{2m}\rvert > \mathbb{E}\lvert q_m\rvert$** hold strictly for **every** integer $m \ge 1$ ($\mathbb{E}\lvert q_2\rvert - \mathbb{E}\lvert q_1\rvert \in [16083/1000000, 4057/250000]$ by a non-oscillatory Parseval $\lambda$-series and 1D separate-convexity Jensen/Edmundson–Madansky reduction, $m = 2\dots 12$ by 1088-bit Hankel quadrature + $C_{1/4}$ tails, and all $m \ge 13$ with $\mathbb{E}\lvert q_{m+1}\rvert - \mathbb{E}\lvert q_m\rvert \ge 1/(25 m(m+1)) > 0$ from strict log-slope increase $g'(\zeta) > 0$ of $g(\zeta) = \zeta^{-1}\ln\psi_{\mathrm{Wick}}(\zeta)$ on $(0, 6/5]$); (ii) *Diffuse unequal-weight doubling:* for any $q_0(X) = \sum_{j=1}^m c_j H(X_j)$ ($u_j = \lvert c_j\rvert^2$, $u = \sum u_j > 0$) with $\max_j u_j \le u/7$, **$\mathbb{E}\lvert(q_0 + q_0')/\sqrt{2}\rvert - \mathbb{E}\lvert q_0\rvert \ge (1/40)(\sum_{j=1}^m u_j^2)/u^{3/2} > 0$**. Numerical evidence on general cubics: three monomials and 40 random cubics in 1–4 complex variables, all with gains 0.016–0.08 (Monte Carlo, 10⁶ samples per case). Near the Gaussian, E\|X\| ≈ (√π/2)‖X‖₂(1 − κ/16) with excess kurtosis κ = E\|X\|⁴/‖X‖₂⁴ − 2 ≥ 0, and doubling halves κ, so the gain is positive to first order; the relation is exact for Gaussian scale mixtures | general H₂,₁ open; equal-weight $q_m$ for all $m \ge 1$ and unequal-weight $\sum c_j H(X_j)$ with $\max u_j \le u/7$ proved; random and near-extremal cubics in 2–8 complex coordinates tested numerically | **open** in general; **P + C, author only** for step-by-step CLT monotonicity $\mathbb{E}\lvert q_{m+1}\rvert > \mathbb{E}\lvert q_m\rvert$ ($m \ge 1$), equal-weight doubling ($m \ge 1$), diffuse unequal-weight doubling ($\max u_j \le u/7$), and self-contained 1088-bit certification of the real $\operatorname{He}_3/\sqrt{6}$ failure ($\mathbb{E}\lvert h_3\rvert \in [3082301/5000000, 6164603/10^7]$, $\mathbb{E}\lvert(h_3 + h_3')/\sqrt{2}\rvert \in [6038107/10^7, 1509527/2500000]$, gain $\in [-25299/2000000, -63247/5000000] < -1/80 < 0$; gemini-21d5, task #730, KWPVD). General numerical evidence **N** (a Claude session of the user, 16 Nov 2026, relayed by the user; replayed by claude-b9ee). The near-extremal cubics gain +0.015 to +0.031, and the Wick monomial +0.0162 (quadrature, claude-b9ee; `[0.016083, 0.016228]` certified in KWPVD). The smallest gain an adversarial search found is +0.0065, on H₂,₁(ℂ²). **The real analogue is false:** X = He₃(G)/√6 has E\|(X+X′)/√2\| = 0.6038108 < E\|X\| = 0.6164602 (GPT, an exact rational certificate replayed by claude-b9ee, quadrature by claude-b9ee, and self-contained closed-form enclosure in KWPVD). | 3.21 | **Routes to a weak radius ρ\* ≤ 0.916 on H₂,₁,** a sufficient condition for the t = 0 radius test of the uncapped degree-5 game (`../higher-degree/STATUS.md` 4.12); **proved on H₂,₁ directly: 2287/2500 = 0.9148 and 2289/2500 = 0.9156** (3.7, **P + C**; **114/125 = 0.912** and **1829/2000 = 0.9145**, **P + C, author only**, and **4571/5000 = 0.9142**, **P, author only + C**; external to Lean). (a) By phase averaging, E\|h\| ≤ √0.68‖h‖₂ on the real third chaos also suffices; equivalently Var\|h\| ≥ 0.32·E h². (b) Moments, hypercontractivity and the cumulant bounds cannot give it. The law Y = (2400/2419)·Unif{±9/10} + (19/2419)·N(0, 25), which is not a chaos law, has E Y² = 1, κ₄ = 748566/60475 ≈ 12.38, κ₆² < 68²κ₄³, even moments of orders 4–18 below those of He₃(G)/√6, ‖Y‖_p ≤ (p − 1)^{3/2} for every p ≥ 2, and positive definite moment matrices M_k(i, j) = (k − ij/(i + j − 1))·E Y^{i+j} for 2 ≤ k ≤ 9 (the author takes them from Azmoodeh–Malicet–Mijoule–Poly, arXiv:1305.6579, Theorem 7; M₁₀ fails); yet E\|Y\| = (2160 + 95√(2/π))/2419 > 0.924. With the atom P(\|Y\| = 5) = 19/2419 in place of the Gaussian component, E\|Y\| = 55/59 > 0.932 under the moment constraints of orders 4 and 6. Products of three independent normal variables are moment-indeterminate (Berg, as cited by the author), and a quadrature-moment relaxation for a sum of four independent He₃ stays above 0.8338 with the moments up to order 254 (**N**, author only). (c) Large kurtosis does not force influential coordinates: κ₄ ≤ 540·λ_max(f⊗₂f), with equality for He₃-sums. (d) A smoothing route. For F in the real third chaos with E F² = 1 and Γ = ‖DF‖²/3: **E\|F\| − √(2/π) = √(2/π)·∫₀^∞C(s)(1 + 2s)^{−3/2}ds, C(s) = E[(Γ − 1)e^{−sF²}]** (the real, unbuffered form of 3.22). The pointwise sign C ≤ 0 would give 3.6; item 95 does not exclude it, and it holds, strictly, on the conditionally Gaussian forms (3.21 (e7)). Other sufficient conditions: 3.21 (e10). The complex constant is π/(2√2) times the real one: 0.915925 for √0.68, and 0.916011 for 0.8247. | 3.21 (e) | **The sharp real bound E\|h\| ≤ √(2/π)‖h‖₂ on the real third chaos.** It gives 3.6 by the phase averaging of (a) and is the d = 3 case of the statement of arXiv:2610.10477, Example 3.1 (cited in 3.6). It is open in general: (e1) to (e6) are the classes on which it is proved, (e7) a stronger sign on one of them, (e8) the numerics, (e9) the weak target √(17/25) and (e10) a sufficient condition from a martingale–isoperimetric bound; h₃ = He₃/√6; X, Y, Z, Xᵢ, Zᵢ and W are independent standard Gaussians unless an item says otherwise, and other letters are local to each item. (e1) **Structure and cumulants.** Cubics odd under one reflection (every degree); conditionally Gaussian forms Σ G_i Q_i(Y); at most seven real coordinates (constant 28√5/(25π) = 0.79717; eleven by radial averaging in (e3)); κ₄ ≥ 49 by (e4); **0 < κ₄ ≤ 1/12250** (and **E\|F\| ≤ √(2/π)‖F‖₂(1 − κ₄/48)** for **0 < κ₄ ≤ 1/49000**); in at most four coordinates also with the constant 79/100. In three coordinates a radial decomposition gives the bound without a computer: E\|P\| ≤ 0.72657‖P‖₂ = 0.9106·√(2/π)‖P‖₂ (supremum at t = 2.2897). The same argument (Cauchy–Schwarz on the sphere, the radial law only) has the constants M_n/√(2/π) = 0.7726 (exact), 0.8710, 0.9106, 0.9324, 0.9562 and 0.9879 for n = 1 to 6, and 1.0134 at n = 7: it could give a second proof for n ≤ 6 (with an interval check for n = 4 to 6, not done) and none beyond. For the tensor projections Π₁^{(m)} ⊗ Π_{d−1}^{(n)} and (Π₁^{(n)})^{⊗d}, d ≥ 2, whose ranges consist of conditionally Gaussian forms, SDP/OPT → π/2 exactly as m, n → ∞ (OPT and SDP as in 3.6): OPT ≤ 2/π by conditional Gaussianity, and SDP ≥ (1 − 1/m)(1 − v_{d−1}/n) with v_k = Var(ψ_k(G)²), ψ_k = He_k/√(k!) (v₂ = 14, v₃ = 92). This is a variant of Example 3.1 that holds. (e2) **Diagonal sums.** For every n and every unit λ ∈ ℝⁿ, E\|Σᵢλᵢh₃(Xᵢ)\| < √(2/π): strict in every dimension, and sharp, since λ = (1, …, 1)/√n gives √(2/π) as n → ∞; the gap is above 0.0087 when max\|λᵢ\| ≥ 1/3. The proof writes √(2/π) − E\|Σᵢλᵢh₃(Xᵢ)\| as a Fourier integral, splits it at t = (29/10)/M with M = max\|λᵢ\|, and bounds the tail with a nonincreasing envelope \|ψ(u)\| ≤ e^{−c(u)u²} of the characteristic function ψ of h₃(X), which makes the tail bound independent of n. What remains is one inequality in κ = (1 − M²)/M², certified on [0, 8] (smallest margin 0.0412) and proved by hand beyond. (e3) **Slicing by great circles; harmonic cubics.** A homogeneous cubic lies in the third chaos iff it is harmonic. Restricted to a Haar-random great circle of a sphere of radius R, F lies in span{cos φ, sin φ, cos 3φ, sin 3φ}, where the angular lemma of the seven-coordinate case holds: ‖g‖₁ ≤ (4√5/(3π))‖g‖₂, with equality exactly at the rotations of sin φ + (1/3)·sin 3φ. With this plain lemma, E\|H\| ≤ θₙ·(4√5/(3π))‖H‖₂ for harmonic H on ℝⁿ, θₙ = E R³/(E R⁶)^{1/2}, R ~ χₙ, below √(2/π) exactly for n ≤ 11 (0.797225 at n = 11, 0.807339 at n = 12). Sharper, the weighted circle lemma: for g in that span with frequency energies e₁, e₃ and every γ ≥ 8/9, (mean\|g\|)² ≤ (8/π²)(1 + 1/(9γ))(e₁ + γe₃), with equality at sin φ + sin 3φ/(3γ), and 8/9 is sharp. A degree-3 spherical harmonic on S^{n−1} keeps on average the fraction τ₃(n) = (n + 2)/(4(n − 1)) of its energy at frequency 3 on a Haar-random great circle, and its cross terms with degree 1 vanish on average. Choosing γ per radius gives E\|F\| ≤ J_n(p)‖F‖₂, with J_n concave in the share p of the degree-1 part. Hence every harmonic cubic in at most 12 coordinates satisfies the sharp bound (b₁₂ = 0.7978796683; exactly, b₁₂²π = 67108041/2²⁵ < 2) and every one in at most 15 satisfies (T) of (e9) (b₁₅ = 0.8218762070); in particular every square-free multilinear cubic in at most 12 variables satisfies the sharp bound. The method stops at n = 13 (0.80689) and n = 16 (0.82818). For the whole chaos, integrate the Gaussian radius before averaging the great-circle means. In the quadratic envelope keep the product of the degree-one part and the first Fourier mode of the harmonic part. This product has zero frame mean. After rotation to align the degree-one part, the envelope is certified on eight facets of the coefficient box. The resulting squared bounds are 253761/400000, 87037/130000 and 38043/56000 at n = 11, 14 and 15, respectively. Embedding lower dimensions gives the Gaussian bound through eleven and (T) through fifteen effective coordinates, the rank of M in (e4). On the 2-sphere: for the 10-dimensional space of odd polynomials of degree at most 3 restricted to S², C = max ‖Y‖₁/‖Y‖₂ is conjectured to be √55/8 = 0.927025, attained at z(9 − 7z²), the projection of the hemisphere sign(z). Proved: C² is the maximum over the sign patterns σ = sign Y of ‖P₁σ‖² + ‖P₃σ‖²; every odd zonal σ with values in [−1, 1] has ‖Pσ‖² ≤ 55/64, with equality only at ±sign(z) (the extreme patterns are σ_c = sign(z(z² − c)), and ‖Pσ_c‖² − 55/64 = −c(1 − c)·[17/64 + (7/16)(5c − 7/2)²] exactly); for max ‖Y‖₁²/(a‖Y₁‖² + b‖Y₃‖²), the hemisphere is the unique zonal maximiser iff b/a > 175/192, and the maximiser over all Y for b/a ≥ 7/2 (Baernstein–Taylor cap symmetrisation; (k + 4)/2 on S^{k−1}); the hemisphere is a strict local maximum (second variation along odd nodal curves: −(5/8)ε²‖η₃‖² at m = 3 and −(45/16)ε²‖η_m‖² for m ≥ 5); on S^{k−1} the hemisphere gives (2(k + 2)(7k + 1)/(3πk(k + 1)²))·(Γ(k/2 + 1)/Γ((k + 1)/2))², which tends to 7/(3π), and it is not optimal for k ≥ 6; the octant sign(xyz) gives ‖Pσ‖² = 105/(16π²) and the sector sign(x³ − 3xy²) gives 315/512. Open: C = √55/8 over all Y. The latitude relaxation of TCH4d (per-latitude Parseval, single-mode Fourier and centroid constraints) cannot prove it: c₀ = v₁ = 0, fixed directions and (\|v₂\|, \|v₃\|) = (17/50, 31/50) on [0, 1/2], the reversed pair on (1/2, 1], satisfy all its constraints, with relaxed energy (105/4)(199/1600)² + (35/8)((130π + 63√3)/1600)² = 0.86379 > 55/64; without the centroid constraint the relaxed maximum is about 0.9866. If the conjecture holds, slicing harmonic cubics by random 3-planes would give the sharp bound for n ≤ 13 and (T) for n ≤ 17 (an estimate). (e4) **A rank-one criterion, and the complete cubics.** Let ‖P‖₂ = 1, let v be a critical point on the unit sphere (a maximiser, say) of f(v) = E[h₃(⟨v, Z⟩)P(Z)], a = f(v), W₁ = ⟨v, Z⟩ and W′ the coordinates of Z in v^⊥. Then P = a·h₃(W₁) + W₁Q₂(W′) + R₃(W′), with no h₂(W₁)L(W′) term (the first-order condition, GPT's TCH `gpt/optimal_direction.tex`, Theorem 1). With b = ‖Q₂‖₂ and c = ‖R₃‖₂ (a² + b² + c² = 1), E\|P\|/√(2/π) ≤ (5/(4√6))·[1 + π(34925a²/131072 + 25b²/64 + c²/2)], so E\|P\| < √(2/π) whenever (30611/131072)a² + (7/64)b² > 1/2 − (4√6 − 5)/(5π) = 0.1945525…; this needs a² > 0.686, so it covers only cubics near one He₃ direction. With (e3) it gives the sharp bound for the normalised elementary symmetric cubic e₃(Z₁, …, Z_N)/C(N, 3)^{1/2} for every N ≥ 3: (e3) for N ≤ 11, the criterion along (1, …, 1)/√N for N ≥ 11. Before, only N ≤ 7 and N ≥ 26 (where κ₄ ≥ 74) were covered. At a maximizing direction take a = maxᵥ \|E[F·h₃(⟨v, X⟩)]\| for unit F and condition on the orthogonal Gaussian coordinates W′. Decoupling q from b in Ψ(q, b) = E\|h₃(W₁) + qW₁ + b\| via a 22-knot even convex 1-Lipschitz piecewise-linear majorant Φ(b) and integrating Φ(R₃(W′)/a) along Gaussian circles in V₃(W′) (where Mehler's identity gives circle-mode variances (3/2)E R₃²/a² and (1/2)E R₃²/a²) gives the Gaussian bound for a ≥ 349/500 = 0.698; bounding Ψ(q, b) on ℝ × [0, ∞) by nineteen separable degree-six rational majorants C + ℓq + μ_q q² + αb + μ_{b,2}b² − μ_{b,4}b⁴ + μ_{b,6}b⁶ with dimension induction on R₃ and the universal third-chaos cumulant inequality \|κ₆(F)\| ≤ 67κ₄(F)^{3/2} (E[F⁶] ≤ 15 + 15κ₄ + 67κ₄^{3/2}, from Lean's `Chaos3.wick6`) gives (T) for a ≥ 321/500 = 0.642. For F = I₃(T)/√6 (‖T‖_HS = 1), write Mᵢⱼ = Σₖₗ TᵢₖₗTⱼₖₗ, λ = ‖M‖ₒₚ, slice matrices (Aᵣ)ᵢⱼ = Tᵣᵢⱼ (Σᵣ Aᵣ² = M), and U_{abcd} = Σᵣ T_{rab}T_{rcd} with full symmetrisation Ũ. Restricting T to the span of a top unit M-eigenvector e₁ and T(e₁,e₁,·)\|_{e₁^⊥} and rotating ℝ² to a circle maximiser proves without a computer the sharp comparison **3λ − 1 ≤ (1 + √2)a²** across all dimensions (attained in n = 2 at T\* = (−3 cos(π/8) e₁⊙e₁⊙e₂ + sin(π/8) e₂^{⊗3})/√(2 + √2/2); slot counting gives λ ≤ (1 + a²)/2, which helps only for a > √((2√2 + 1)/7) ≈ 0.7395). At a = 321/500 = 0.642 this gives λ ≤ (1 + (1 + √2)(321/500)²)/3 < 33251/50000 = 0.66502. Choosing e₂ ⊥ e₁ along T(e₁,e₁,·)\|_{e₁^⊥} with x = T₁₁₁ ≥ 0, s = T₁₁₂ ≥ 0, B = T(e₁,·,·)\|_{e₁^⊥} and C = T(e₂,·,·)\|_{e₁^⊥}, coupling M₁₂ = 0, symmetrised directional caps on B, Weyl–Karamata bounds on Tr M² and the exact defect identity **90 Tr M² − κ₄ = 54‖U − Ũ‖²_HS = 18 Σ_{r,s}‖AᵣAₛ − AₛAᵣ‖²_F ≥ 72‖s²e₂e₂ᵀ + B² − xB − sC‖²_HS** proves κ₄ < 46.6 when a ≤ 0.642 and λ < 0.692, κ₄ < 49 when a ≤ 0.698; hence (T) holds for a ≥ 0.642, λ ≥ 0.66502 or κ₄ ≥ 46.6, and the Gaussian bound holds for a ≥ 0.698, λ ≥ 0.692 or κ₄ ≥ 49, confining any counterexample outside (e1)–(e6) for the sharp bound to rank M ≥ 12, a < 0.698, λ < 0.692 and κ₄ < 49. (e5) **Four-partite cubics, and Lemma (L).** One block: E\|aX + bY + cXY\| ≤ √(2/π)·(a² + b² + c²)^{1/2}, with equality iff c = 0; at unit variance the gap is at least 0.078c². Lemma (L): for independent standard Gaussian vectors X ∈ ℝ^m, Y ∈ ℝⁿ and Z₀ ∈ ℝ, g = ⟨u, X⟩ + ⟨v, Y⟩ + ⟨X, MY⟩ + c₀Z₀ has E\|g\| ≤ √(2/π)‖g‖₂, with equality iff M = 0; equivalently, the bound holds for every element of the first two chaoses whose second-chaos part has a spectrum symmetric about 0. Its proof conditions twice, which leaves one block, and applies Jensen. Consequences: every 4-partite cubic (a sum of trilinear forms, one for each three of four independent Gaussian vectors), strictly unless it vanishes; every ⟨u(ω), X₃⟩ + ⟨v(ω), X₄⟩ + ⟨X₃, M(ω)X₄⟩ with square-integrable u, v, M of a random element ω independent of the Gaussian vectors X₃, X₄, which on the third chaos means every cubic that, for some orthogonal split ℝ^N = A ⊕ B₃ ⊕ B₄, has in each monomial at most one variable of B₃, at most one of B₄ and at least one of B₃ ∪ B₄ (variables of A may repeat); and every (d + 1)-partite element of the chaos of order d, for every d ≥ 2. A 4-partite cubic without one of the four triples is conditionally Gaussian, hence in (e1); with all four, no part meets every monomial exactly once (whether some member has such a structure after a rotation was not checked), and the class has members in any number of coordinates (blow-ups and sums of copies of K₄^(3), tight cycles C_n^(3) with 3 ∤ n, n ≥ 7). Also κ₃² ≤ (3/4)κ₄ for every unit-variance element of the first two chaoses. Not proved: the sharp bound on all of the first two chaoses, Σᵢ(λᵢ(gᵢ² − 1) + μᵢgᵢ); numerically the normalised gap (1 − E\|Q\|/(√(2/π)‖Q‖₂))/κ₄ has infimum about 1/96, at the Gaussian limit. (e6) **The Gaussian-bath family.** F = a·He₃(X) + b·XY + c·Z with a, b, c real: the limit, in law and in first absolute moment, of the third-chaos variables a·He₃(X) + bX·Σᵢ(Uᵢ² − 1)/√(2N) + c·ΣᵢHe₃(Vᵢ)/√(6N), sums over i ≤ N, a Gaussian-bath limit of the shape of the numerical maximisers of TCH `R3_REPORT.md`. E\|F\| ≤ √(2/π)‖F‖₂, with equality iff a = b = 0. The family only: no bound in finitely many coordinates follows. With p = 6a² and q = b² at unit variance the proof uses Jensen on the edge p = 0, a weighted majorant on 3p + 5q ≥ 1, corner bounds by hand for p ≤ 1/9 and for 1/5 < p < 1/3, and an interval certificate for 1/9 < p ≤ 1/5. The weaker E\|F\| ≤ (5/(2√(3π)))‖F‖₂ = 0.8143‖F‖₂, which gives the weak form (a) on the family with room, comes from E\|μ + σW\| ≤ √(μ² + (2/π)σ²) given X and a Gaussian weight that makes the bound affine in (6a², b², c²). Closure: if a family is closed under adding an independent Gaussian and satisfies the sharp bound, the sum of a member and any independent Gaussian scale mixture satisfies it too (condition on the scale, then Jensen). Re((X₁ + iX₂)³)/√24 is exactly such a mixture, √((9/2)V₁V₂)·G with V₁ ~ Gamma(1/3) and V₂ ~ Gamma(2/3) (shapes; unit scale) and G ~ N(0, 1), all independent (Mellin transforms and Gauss's triplication formula; the same argument applies to Re(zᵏ) for every k). Hence F plus any independent conditionally Gaussian forms and blocks Re((X₁ + iX₂)³) satisfies the sharp bound. Unimodal replacement: for every symmetric unimodal W independent of X, E\|a·h₃(X) + W\| ≤ E\|aZ + W\|. Khintchine's representation of W as a mixture of uniform laws reduces this to E(\|h₃(X)\| − M)₊² ≥ E(\|Z\| − M)₊² for every M > 0, which follows from a single sign change of D₀(w) = E(\|Z\| − w)₊ − E(\|h₃(X)\| − w)₊, from + to −, near w = 1.405. (e7) **The smoothing sign on conditionally Gaussian forms.** For P = Σ_{i∈A} Z_iQ_i(Z_B) with disjoint coordinate sets A and B (A meets every monomial exactly once), E P² = 1 and V = ΣᵢQᵢ², the function C of (d) is C(s) = Cov(V, (1 + 2sV)^{−1/2}), negative for every s > 0 unless V is constant. At s = 1/2 this says that the heat-flow deficit E[(3 − \|∇P\|²)e^{−P²/2}] equals −3·Cov(V, (1 + V)^{−1/2}) > 0. The proof applies Sherman–Morrison to the law of Z_A given Z_B tilted by e^{−sP²}; the terms in \|∇V\|² then cancel by the carré du champ of the second chaos. (e8) **Numerics and literature.** The largest values of E\|h\|/(√(2/π)‖h‖₂) found are 0.7726 (n = 1, exact), 0.7862, 0.8279, 0.8423, 0.8589 and 0.8755 for n = 2 to 6, and 0.9051 at n = 10 (lower bounds on the suprema); no counterexample was found. No proof for any degree d ≥ 2 was found in the literature. Directly on H₂,₁, 2289/2500 = 0.9156 < 0.916 is proved outside Lean (3.7, **P + C**). (i) For every nonzero F, E\|F\| < (44357/50000)‖F‖₂ = 0.88714‖F‖₂. (e10) **A sufficient condition from a martingale–isoperimetric bound.** For unit P in the chaos of odd order d, with N_t(x) = E sign P(√t·x + √(1 − t)·G) for an independent standard Gaussian vector G, I(s) = φ(Φ⁻¹((1 + s)/2)) (the Gaussian isoperimetric profile), μ(t) = E I(N_t), W̄_P = ∫₀¹ d·t^{d−1}·I(N_t)/μ(t) dt and Γ_P = \|∇P\|²/d: E\|P\| ≤ B(P) ≤ R(P) = √(2/π)·(E[W̄_PΓ_P])^{1/2}, where B(P) = ∫₀¹ (−μ′(t))^{1/2}·(8t^{d−1}·E[I(N_t)\|∇P\|²])^{1/2} dt. Since E W̄_P = E Γ_P = 1, R(P)² = (2/π)(1 + Cov(W̄_P, Γ_P)), so the sharp bound on the chaos of order d follows from Ω_d: Cov(W̄_P, Γ_P) ≤ 0 for every unit P. The proof runs the martingales t^{d/2}P(β_t/√t) and N_t(β_t/√t) along a Brownian motion (β_t) on [0, 1], then uses Cauchy–Schwarz with the weight I(N_t), the identity I·I″ = −1/4, and Cauchy–Schwarz in t. W̄_P is large near the nodal set {P = 0}: as t → 1, the scale-wise covariance Cov(I(N_t), Γ_P)/μ(t) tends to S(P) − 1, where S(P) = ∫_{P=0} Γ_P dσ_γ / ∫_{P=0} dσ_γ is the average of Γ_P over the Gaussian surface measure σ_γ of {P = 0}, and not to g_P(0) − 1 with the zero-density quantity g_P(0) = E[Γ_P \| P = 0] of TCH3a §5.2. The surface quotient is the definition: it equals E[Γ_P^{3/2} \| P = 0]/E[Γ_P^{1/2} \| P = 0] only when the coarea law of P is regular at 0; for P = XYZ the density of P at 0 is infinite, both conditional moments tend to 0, and S = 1/3. The limit is the local half-space limit at the regular points of {P = 0}, extended across the singular set and to the Gaussian tails by the tube-volume estimate for real algebraic sets of Basu and Lerario (arXiv:2104.05053, Theorem 1.1). Ω_d is a sufficient condition next to that zero-density condition and the sign C ≤ 0 of (d), which (e7) proves on conditionally Gaussian forms. For h₃ in one variable, E\|h₃\| = 0.616460 ≤ B = 0.772382 ≤ R = 0.789804 < √(2/π), with Cov = −0.020151. Numerically Cov < 0 for about 200 cubics (F_N up to N = 300, diagonal sums, the two-variable chaos, the Gaussian-bath family, e₃(N), the Fano cubic, random multilinear cubics); away from the Gaussian limit the largest value is that of h₃, a strict local maximum in every direction tested, and for F_N and e₃(N), 8 ≤ N ≤ 20 (in the window n ≥ 8, 0.027 < κ₄ < 74 of the report), the values lie between −0.15 and −0.013. | 3.23 | **Rotation identity.** For q ∈ H₂,₁, V = \|q\|², c₁ = Σ_j\|∂\_jq\|², c₂ = Σ_j\|∂̄\_jq\|² and J = c₁ − c₂ (E c₁ = 2u, E c₂ = u): **E f(V) − f(0) = E[J·f′(V)]** for every f ∈ C² of polynomial growth; on H_{p,p′} the left side carries the factor p − p′. It is the difference of the two forms of 6.3.20 with G = q̄γ(V), so it contains no term in a. Consequences. (a) E[J \| V = v]·p_V(v) = P(V > v): on the law of V alone it says nothing, and what it adds is the pointwise representative J. (b) D_φ = −Cov(J, K′(V)) for the profiles of 6.3.24, and every split obtained from 6.3.20 with G = q̄γ(V) is [D − s·T₂] + s·T₂, where T₂ = E[Re(q̄²a)γ′(V)] (6.3.24 is s = 1, the real Stein identity s = 2/3): that family has one free term. (c) The Stein kernel of F = √(2/u)·Re q is E[J \| F]/u; hence the function of 3.22 is C_F(α) = E[(J/u − 1)·ι(αV/u)] with ι(t) = e^{−t}I₀(t), and u·C_F(α) = E[(V − u)ι(t) + V·e^{−t}I₁(t)] at t = αV/u. (d) With N(λ) = (1 + λu)·E e^{−λV} − 1 = −λ·Cov(J, e^{−λV}), the deficit at the Gaussian buffer σ is √π·√(σ² + u) − 2E\|σW + q\| = π^{−1/2}∫₀^{1/σ²}N(λ)·λ^{−3/2}(1 − λσ²)^{1/2}(1 + λu)⁻¹dλ, and u·C_F(α) = −π⁻¹∫₀^{2α/u}N(λ)·λ^{−3/2}(2α/u − λ)^{−1/2}dλ. So the Laplace order (N ≥ 0), the buffer deficits and the smoothing sign of 3.22 are positive functionals of one function N | every dimension. N is negative at large λ for some cubics (item 5), so no sign follows from (d) alone | **P, author only** (a research worker of claude-b9ee, 16 Nov 2026; every step re-derived by hand by claude-b9ee, which is the same session, so the ledger counts it as the author's) + **C, author only** on seven random rational cubics, n ≤ 3 (replayed by claude-b9ee, identical logs); for f = K the identity holds to 10⁻⁷ in an independent quadrature (**N**, claude-b9ee) | KDS `REPORT.md` §2, `REVIEW_claude-b9ee.md` §§1–3; `code/deficit-sign-2026-11-16/x01_exact_identities.py` |

| 3.26a | **Sharp covariance comparator for the decoupled term.** For a holomorphic quadratic field $F$ with $H=\mathbb E FF^*$ and nonzero energy $u=\operatorname{tr}H$, $\mathbb E e^{-t\Vert F\Vert^2}>\det(I+tH)^{-1}$ for $t>0$. The centered Gaussian vector with covariance $H$ gives the sharp supremum for Bernstein functions of the squared norm. For zero-contraction cubics with output rank at most $r\ge1$, the supremum of $\mathbb E\lvert q\rvert/\sqrt u$ over all finite input dimensions is $\sqrt\pi\,\Gamma(r+1/2)/(2\sqrt r\,\Gamma(r))$. This is $\pi/4$ at rank one. Each supremum is approached as the input dimension grows and is strict for every individual nonzero cubic. The peak-row decoupled deficit exceeds $\operatorname{tr}H^2/8$ for $u\le463/10000$. | Arbitrary finite input and output dimensions. | 3.30 | **The complex quintic chaos H₃,₂(ℂⁿ).** (a) Normalised diffuse sums Q_N = N^{−1/2}Σ_{j≤N}q₁(z_j) (q₁(z) = z(\|z\|⁴ − 6\|z\|² + 6)/√12, χ = 1840/N) and multilinear sums M_K = K^{−1/2}Σ_{r≤K}z_{5r−4}z_{5r−3}z_{5r−2}z̄_{5r−1}z̄_{5r} (χ = 30/K) converge in L¹ to √π/2, so sup_{n,q≠0} E\|q\|/‖q‖₂ ≥ √π/2. The normalisation agrees with ours (**N**, REV §3): the visible profile is L = Z(0.66165 + 0.16917\|Z\|²), ratio 0.2557
against 0.2561 at the peak, and the supremum of Φ over Gaussian remote q is 0.7131355 at E\|q\|² = 0.0572, the known
model norm. So 11.7 would close the remote part with a loss of 1.15·10⁻⁴, just below the threshold 500/701 for 1.402.
Cubics sharing z₁ are outside these claims.

| 6.5.1 | Gaussian-marginal obstruction: at the peak, Q = √u₀·X·exp(2πi·F₂(\|X\|²)) with F₂(r) = 1 − (r + 1)e^{−r} and u₀ = 403448/10⁷ has exactly the law of √u₀·W, is orthogonal to the whole remote first chaos and has the matched conditional covariance profile; with t₀ = 1/1250, G = Q − i·t₀(T − 1)X satisfies Λ(G) > S + 7·10⁻⁷. ### 7.5 All sectors together

At the peak row, with the remote trial ξ₀ = −(3/5)(r_A + 0.5053r_B) of 7.4.10 and one global visible coefficient c,
F_ξ₀(G) = ‖ξ₀‖² + \|c\|² + E\|L + G − ξ₀ − cz₁\| + E\|L − G + ξ₀ + cz₁\| − p‖G‖² bounds Λ̂(G) from above for
G = q + (T − 1)r_A + z₁²·conj(r_B)/√2 + z₁Q + z̄₁P + ν·z₁(T − 2)/√2, with energies u = ‖q‖², V = ‖r_A‖² + ‖r_B‖², W_Q,
W_P, W_ν and W = W_Q + W_P + W_ν.

| 7.5.13 | **Global all-sector pointwise closure $F_{\mathrm{joint}}(c^*) \le 0$ on all of $\mathbb{C}^6$ and unconditional six-sector bound at the peak row.** At the peak row, with the global $U(1)$-invariant visible shift $c^* = -(7/25)\nu$ and global zero-mean compensating form $C_{\mathrm{inv}}(Q, \nu) = -(6801/18910)\Re(Q\bar{\nu})$ ($\mathbb{E}C_{\mathrm{inv}} = 0$ identically from $\mathbb{E}Q = 0$ and deterministic $\nu$; giving centered odd quadratic budget $B_{\mathrm{odd}} = \operatorname{diag}(B_3, B_3)$ with off-diagonal $k_{\mathrm{comp}} = -11/20$, $d_\nu = 3422717/2363750$, and $\lambda_{\min}(B_{\mathrm{odd}}) > 50/61$), the centred joint defect satisfies **$F_{\mathrm{joint}}(c^*)(q, A, B, Q, P, \nu) \le 0$ for every $(q, A, B, Q, P, \nu) \in \mathbb{C}^6$**, with no $|\nu|^2 \le 1/50$ cap and no restriction on phases, amplitudes, or sharing. **Immediate corollary** (via 7.5.6, 3.3, 3.14 [**C**], and `KAST`): every admissible six-sector tuple $G$ at the peak row satisfying $u \le 0.0463$ and $u + V \le 0.080051$ (arbitrary ambient dimension, arbitrary remote sharing, and arbitrary odd energy $W \ge 0$) obeys **$\widehat{\Lambda}(G) < S + 29.36\cdot 10^{-6}$** (`D_+` 11.61 alone, strict $< S$ on $u \in [0, 0.03]$) and **$\widehat{\Lambda}(G) < S + 27.00\cdot 10^{-6}$** (signed 11.61 on $[2/125, 0.0463]$, strict $< S$ on $u \in [0, 0.0354] \cup [0.0456, 0.0463]$; `< S + 4936/10^8` under the original 7.4.10 allocation). Proof reduces by exact $U(1)$ rotation and complex conjugation to $q = w \ge 0$, $\Im A \ge 0$, introduces the sharp anisotropic capped 2-sign inequality $|X + O'| + |X - O'| - 2|X| \le \psi(|X|, q) + (O')^T A(X, q) O'$ with $\psi(r, q) = q^2/(\mu(\mu + r)^2)$ and $A(X, q) = \mu^{-1}I_2 - X X^T/(\mu(\mu^2 + r^2))$ ($\mu = \sqrt{r^2 + q}$, $q > 0$; For the raw six-sector payoff, outside the small-energy region of 7.5.1, no theorem covers G = q + A + B + odd sectors with q ≠ 0:
Theorem A needs q = 0 and Theorem B Gaussian drivers with a nonnegative profile. Needed: the joint A/B bound (7.4),
keeping the joint law of q and its drivers; the raw A-content for general cubics (6.3.18), which 7.1.4 and 7.1.6
turn into B-content; a bound for the odd sectors over cores with A/B shells, averaged over the drivers (7.2.4;
over remote cores it is 7.2.10). Numerically
(11.45) the pointwise remainder bound of the tilted A-sector (11.39) has analogues for A and B together and for A, B,
z₁Q and z̄₁P, but not with d·z₁(T − 2): its cross term with z₁Q vanishes after integration at the zero core (EQ = 0),
while at a nonzero core it has two coupling channels (7.2.5; item 77).

Thresholds: 1.402 needs a row cap below 500/701 = 0.7132667617…, and 1.4022 below 5000/7011 = 0.7131650263…
(REC §6). The partial consumers are rows 8.5 to 8.13. The full consumer is not built (**open**): it needs an exact cover of the response parameter t and of the
rows; the σ\* = 0 corner (2.5), or a proof that the cover avoids it; interval upper bounds for S at each row; coverage
of the energies outside R and of every omitted response region (with the t-dependent caps of 2.12a and 2.12b); an error ledger
with exact thresholds; and, if rows with different ε are used, their relation to a single witness operator (pointwise
bounds for different operators cannot be minimised silently; REC §5). No `lower_of_K`, `KHyp`, `ConjectureK` or
`K_eta` exists in `Grothe/`, `GrotheKernel/`, `docs/LEAN_DETAILS.md` or `docs/overview.tex`.

with zeros). Numbers of OPT are quoted only where a second implementation reproduces them.

A numerical ratio does not measure the distance to a proof: a verified all-dimensional content theorem with constant
0.10 would still leave the joint estimate, the odd sectors, the response regions and the consumer (ASM §5).

## 10. Identity-tail games (N unless marked)

TAIL; scripts in `code/tailgames-2026-11-14/`. Floating point, two-variable graded model; nothing is a certificate.

- A game λ = (1, λ₁, …, λ_D, ν, ν, …) with a constant tail is a valid witness and adds no order parameter, because
  the identity part is pointwise: Re⟨f, g⟩ = 1 − 2‖k‖². Model norms of such games have no truncation error
  (**P, author only**; for the cubic head, `../identity-tail/STATUS.md` 1.1 and 1.2).
- Cubic head with tail: N(1, −ε, ν, ν, …) = (1 − ν)·sup[V_ε̂(f, g) − 2ν̂‖k‖²] + ν, ε̂ = (ε + ν)/(1 − ν),
  ν̂ = ν/(1 − ν) (**P, author only**). The row is the cubic one with the pointwise potential \|A\| + \|B\| replaced by
  Ψ_ν(A, B) = max_{\|u\|=\|v\|=1}[Re(ūA) + Re(v̄B) + ν·Re(uv̄)] = min{(d₁ + d₂ + d₃)/2 : diag(d) ⪰ M},
  M = [[0, A, B], [Ā, 0, ν], [B̄, ν, 0]], with ν̂ in place of ν in the row (the dual formula checked to 5·10⁻¹⁵ on 40
  random cases).
- Optimum ε = 0.7681, ν = +0.0235: model norm **0.713021**, that is 1.402483, against 0.7131355 (1.402258) without a
  tail; two optimisers agree to 10⁻⁶. At degree 5 a tail gains 4.8·10⁻⁵, with ν < 0 (`../higher-degree/STATUS.md` 1.3).
- The certificate route for this witness (the row pipeline with Ψ_ν, the corrected cap majorant, the integer certificate
  and the Lean generalisation) is recorded in `../identity-tail/STATUS.md`, at (ε, ν) = (191/250, 47/2000), the point
  chosen against the relaxed law (1.4 there). No analogue of K for Ψ_ν is formulated.

### 11 (b) The tilted A-sector

At s = 3/5, 𝒥(q, r) is the gain of the tilted A-sector over a remote base: by 11.12,
Λ̂(q + (T − 1)r) − Λ(q) ≤ 𝒥(q, r). Below, f(w, ρ) = E_{z₁}\|L + w + (T − 2/5)ρ\| for complex w, ρ, and
Rem(w, ρ) = 2[f(w, ρ) − f(w, 0) − ⟨∇_ρf(w, 0), ρ⟩].

| 11.56 | **Perturbations of a symmetric cubic.** Let p₀ ∈ H₂,₁ be fixed by a finite diagonal symmetry without fixed coordinates, and let h ∈ H₂,₁ be arbitrary (no independence). If no character of h equals a coordinate character or its negative ("no resonant character"), then ‖c_{3/5}(p₀ + h)‖² ≤ E\|h\|⁴/4 ≤ 11(E\|h\|²)². In general ‖c_{3/5}(p₀ + h)‖ ≤ √(3v_r/2) + √11·v, with v = E\|h\|² and v_r the energy of the resonant part of h. For the five-cycle (weights 1, 2, 4, 8, 16 mod 31) the 75 Wick monomials split into 5 invariant, 35 protected and 35 resonant. With 11.40: Λ̂(p₀ + h + (T − 1)r) < S + 9.2·10⁻⁶ for protected h with v ≤ 1/2000, for every r | the peak row, tilt 3/5; uses \|D²Φ₀[v, v]\| ≤ \|v\|² (from sup N_Φ < 1/3, 11.30) and Lip Φ₀ < √(3/2) | **P + C** (codex-a786; re-derived and replayed by claude-b9ee); inputs 3.3, 3.4, 11.30, 11.40 | KPH `PROOFS.md` §§4, 6 |

| 11.60 | **The Clifford cubic.** q = conj(X₀)·Σ_{a≤8} Y_a² + Σ_{i≤7} X_i·Y\*A_iY ∈ H₂,₁(ℂ¹⁶), with seven Hermitian, antisymmetric, pairwise anticommuting 8×8 matrices A_i (Pauli words). Then \|YᵀY\|² + Σ_i(Y\*A_iY)² = \|Y\|⁴, so q has the law of R·W with R ~ Gamma(8, 1) independent of a standard complex Gaussian W: u = 72, δ = 5472, χ = 19/18, and ℬ = 31392 = (109/19)δ. Every content of q vanishes, for every profile: the unitary maps Y → A_kY, X₀ → −X₀, X_i → −X_i (i ≠ k) fix q and have no common fixed coordinate vector (11.31a with a non-diagonal group). At an interior Gaussian optimiser this gives a strict local maximum in each fixed finite coordinate block, with a neighbourhood that may depend on the dimension; H may not depend on W | **P, author only** (codex-c7e2; reviewed only within its own session) | C7E2 `all-sector/GAUSSIAN_LOCAL_STABILITY.md` |

Hence one χ\* > 0 such that χ(G) = (E\|G\|⁴ − 2U²)/U² ≤ χ\* implies Λ_p(G) ≤ sup_v(B(v) − pv) for every cubic G (all visible sectors, arbitrary sharing, every amplitude), uniformly over a ∈ [11/25, 33/50], b ∈ [3/25, 4/25], p ≥ 25/19. (ii) codex-4d8a, at the peak: \|u − v\*\| < r, 0 < δ < δ₀ and e = ‖h‖² < e₀ imply S − Λ_p(q + h) ≥ c₁(u − v\*)² + c₂δ + c₃e, with a first variation of order δ^{3/4}√e; it uses the remote deficit of 3.18 and the coercivity of the Gaussian visible Hessian. Scope: every dimension, arbitrary sharing; no threshold has a numerical value: codex-c7e2 did not evaluate its own (it is at most of order 10⁻¹⁶ by claude-b9ee's estimate from the stated constants), and codex-4d8a's inherits the unstated constant of 3.18. So neither can enter a certificate, but a counterexample to K cannot be near-Gaussian in these senses | **P, author only** (codex-c7e2 and codex-4d8a, each reviewed only within its own session; claude-b9ee read both structures, not line by line, and replayed their exact checks) | C7E2R2 `FULL_NEAR_GAUSSIAN_K.md`; K4D8A `remote-dependence/UNIFORM_LOCAL_K.md` |

## 13. False statements

Each is false as stated; none is a counterexample to K. Format: the statement, the counterexample, its status, the
source.

1, 2.4. **K for every ε and every profile** (equivalently, same-norm domination on every variance sphere). False for
profiles with ℓ − 2β of order 10⁻¹⁰ to 10⁻³⁰ at tiny norms; no violation is known on the box. **R** (explicit examples
in the notes received). MON §1; LOG §8a, §10; `RCV/chatgpt-thread2-gaussian_domination_note.tex`.

2\. **Constant-free same-norm domination (K\*) for every affine profile.** False where the profile has a zero: for L = z₁(T − 4)
and G_s = i·s·z₁(T − 2) the adversary gains of order s²log(1/s) against the Gaussian's s², for all small s; for L = z₁T
a second example reverses only below s ≈ 4·10⁻⁵. Both profiles are outside the box. **R** (quadrature in the log).
LOG §8, §8a.

3\. **E|L + G| ≤ E|L + σW| (one term alone).** False: G can cancel the cubic part of L. **R**. LOG §8.

4\. **Fibrewise domination at a fixed shift, E|r + q| ≤ E|r + ‖q‖W|.** False even for cubics: for q = z₂(|z₃|² − 1) (unit
norm, conditionally Gaussian) the difference E|r + q| − E|r + W| is −0.234 at r = 0, +2.2·10⁻³ at r = 2.5 and +3.1·10⁻³
at r = 3, and positive for all r ≥ 2.5; only the T-averaged form can hold. **N** (quadrature, claude-b9ee; the three
values reproduced by a second implementation in the records audit). MON §2; LOG §11b, §11g; `ch29`.

5\. **Single-scale strengthenings of K** (heat kernel, Laplace transform, characteristic function, convex order
|q|² ≥_cx ‖q‖²·Exp(1)). False: E e^{−30|H|²} < 1/31 for H = z[(|z|² − 2) + i(|w|² − 1)]/√3 (certified). **R**. MON §2;
LOG §11b; DIG §3; `OCT/prompts/PROMPT_REPLY_K_REFORMULATION.md`.

6\. **Monotonicity along the CLT, Λ(G) ≤ Λ(symmetrised G).** False: G = c·z₁(|z₂|² − 1) gains +4.9·10⁻⁴ at c = 0.15.
**N**. `OCT/pipeline-kclt/RESULTS.md`; DIG §2; LOG §7.

7\. **OU-path monotonicity in its scalar Stein-sign form.** It is not equivalent to monotonicity, and it fails for
G = M·z₁(T − 2) with M large. **R**. LOG §7, §7a; MON §3.3.

8\. **Lemma (T): (𝒞_{F_A}(q) + 𝒞_{F_B}(q))·λ_max(M(q)) ≤ 0.292(S − Λ(q) + η).** False for q = (7/(20√2))X²Ȳ, whose
actual contents vanish; the usable form (T₀) uses the actual contents. **R**.
`OCT/conjecture-K-report/WEIGHTED_CONTENT_FOR_GPT.md`; DIG §2; `ch15`.

10\. **Λ is concave in the conditional covariance parameters** (Jensen for conditionally Gaussian G with a conditional
cross-covariance). False: the largest Hessian eigenvalue is +1.3 to +2.9. **N**. DIG §2; `OCT/pipeline-kclt/RESULTS.md`.

11\. **Replacing a shared (T − 1)U by an independent Gaussian of the same variance does not lower Λ** (equal-variance
peeling). False: shared minus independent reaches +1.96·10⁻² at aligned phase and reverses at first order. **R**.
DIG §3; LOG §11e.

19\. **The tied local matrix of the retained functional, plus I/10, is a global majorant.** False by more than
97/1000 at q = 3/4, α = 7/20, β = 1/2, ξ = 1/5 (interval check); an isotropic 0.8·I is not a majorant even
infinitesimally (normalised curvature above 0.843 in the direction (2, −1)). So the local matrix 11.4 is not a cost.
**R**. DIG §3; `ch25-parts/20-stability-and-smoothing.tex`; `OCT/weak-k-2026-10-15/LOCAL_CHANNELS.md` §§2, 5.

20, 6.4.1. **The Wick monomial maximises C²/D (Conjecture E), or the ratio is monotone toward it.** False: along
q_τ = √(u/(2 + τ²))·X[(|X|² − 2) + iτ(|Y|² − 1)] the derivative of the ratio in τ² at 0 exceeds 1/1000 at
u = 463/10000 (peak); the monomial is a saddle (6.4.2). Second-variation implication **P**, exact algebra **C** (audit);
the sign rests on kernel inputs that are **R**; the finite perturbation **N**. REP §1; CERT §6;
`RCV/progress_2026-11-05/`.

26\. **Content or deficit is Schur-convex in the spectrum of a Hermitian form.** False: (2, −1, −1) ≺ (2, 0, −2), yet
the leading content tensor T(q_λ) = 4(Σ_jλ_j³)·X of q_λ = X·Σ_jλ_j(|Y_j|² − 1) has ‖T‖² = 576 against 0; for the
deficit the record has the assertion only. **R**. LOG §12f, §12h.

27\. **Averaging the phase of X removes the shells X̄B and X²W̄.** False: cross terms survive (6.3.8). **P + C**.
LOG §12f, §15; CERT §8.

28, 6.5.3. **Centring and conditional Gaussianity give the content bound 0.24 without the chaos moment inequality.**
False: two-point laws reach 0.2469 and 0.2646. **R**. LOG §15.

36\. **Every sector other than A is pure cost.** False for B at small amplitude (7.1.2). **P**. ASM §3.1; LOG §31.

37\. **The odd sectors have content of order u.** False: they have no first-order term at all (2.9). **P**. LOG §34.

38\. **Over a nonzero core the odd sectors satisfy E[|O|²/|L + H|] ≤ (1/ε − κ_E)‖O‖².** False even with κ_E = 0:
E[|O|²/|L + H|] > 2.714 > 2.644 = ‖O‖²/ε for O = z₁(|Y|² − 1) + z̄₁Y²/√2 (‖O‖² = 2) at an explicit core (u = 0.0462,
w_A = w_B = 0.0112); a certificate must keep the directional curvature (7.2.2). A second witness, inside the
response-feasible region: item 48. **R**. LOG §36; `RCV/odd_sector_inverse_norm_obstruction/`.

39, 7.3.5. **The B-cost 7.1.1 holds at every even L² base, so it applies at q + (T − 1)r_A.** False: the cusp
H = L·sign(Re z₁); and with a loss of 0.07 on both diagonals the excess rises from 5.5·10⁻⁵ (item 42) to
0.000424157 > 10⁻⁴ at an exact witness (M = [[0.17, 0.118], [0.118, 0.19]], c_A = 0.0144, c_B = 0.5053c_A,
Δ = c_A²/0.24). Exact algebra **P** (audit, replayed); the kernel counterexamples of the package **R**. ASM §3.4;
LOG §43, §45; `RCV/J3_audit/`.

43, 7.4.3. **Separate A and B axis costs imply a joint cost** (so a content constant 0.10 with the diagonal costs
closes A + B). False: 2c_Aa + 2c_Bb − 0.24a² − 0.26b² + 0.40ab with c_A = 1/200, c_B = 0.5053c_A, Δ = 1/4000 satisfies
both axis costs, c_A² = 0.10Δ, exact alignment and both amplitude caps, and exceeds Δ by 0.00033415 > 10⁻⁴. **P**
(exact arithmetic, audit); not a cubic counterexample. ASM §2; REP §2 (a), §4.

7.4.4. **A positive mixed coefficient m makes 2m·Re⟨r_A, r_B⟩ nonnegative.** False for arbitrary relative phases. **P**.
ASM §2, §4.2.

44\. **g_B/g ∈ [0.505, 0.56] at the three rows.** False at the flank and upper rows: the ratio at v = 0 is 0.502632 and
0.501130 there (0.505341 at the peak; certified in the package). At the peak the common-centre comparison 7.1.4
holds. **R**. LOG §45; `RCV/J3_audit/`.

45\. **‖Π(q·h)‖ ≤ E[|q|·h(|q|²)]** (an L¹ projection bound). False in general: for q = y(|y|² − 2)/√2 and h = 1{v > 1}, |q| > 1 forces |y|² > 2, so
‖Π(qh)‖ = E[|q|·|y|·h] ≥ √2·E[|q|·h] (**P**: the records audit; re-derived by claude-b9ee). The valid step is the L² contraction
‖Π(qh)‖₂ ≤ ‖qh‖₂ (**P**). REP §3 item 6.

46\. **The first variation of the B shell pairs with r_B.** False: it pairs with conj(r_B); the pairing with r_B has
charge two and vanishes. **P**. LOG §28; ASM §3.1.

47\. **Decoupling of the odd sectors from the even core** ("M1 decoupling" of the October plan, which calls the sectors
z₁Q, z̄₁P, d·z₁(T − 2) even): Λ(H + O) − Λ(H) ≤ Λ(O + gU) − Λ(gU) for an even core H, odd sectors O and a Gaussian buffer
gU. False by up to +5.7·10⁻³ in 40 random six-sector trials. **N**. `OCT/PLAN_1402.md` ("Negative results").

48\. **Three sufficient conditions of the October 1.402 plan for the cost of the odd sectors O.** (EC): Λ is concave
along the odd sectors at every base G, d²/ds² Λ(G + sO) ≤ 0. (PH): E[|O|²/|L + H|] ≤ p‖O‖² over an even core H, which is
item 38 with κ_E = 0. (PG): 2E[√(|L + H|² + |O|²) − |L + H|] ≤ p‖O‖². (EC) is false as stated: at G = −c·z₁(|X|² − 1),
c > 0, and O = i·z₁(|X|² − 1) the second derivative at s = 0 is +∞. (PH) is false inside the response-feasible region:
at the core 0.15·X(|X|² − 2)/√2 + 0.1·(T − 1)X the averaged matrix has largest eigenvalue 1.3395 > p, so the route
through (PG) and (PH) fails there. **R**. `OCT/PLAN_1402.md` ("M1 status").

49\. **A bound for the matrix of (PH) that is uniform over the fibres** (the remote values held fixed). None exists: at
the zero fibre its largest eigenvalue is 1.7095 > p, through the cross term of z₁Q with d·z₁(T − 2), which E Q = 0
removes only after integration (item 77); and the supremum over the fibres is infinite, where L + H has degenerate
zeros (7.2.4). **R**. `OCT/PLAN_1402.md` ("M1 status").

50\. **The first-chaos penalty evaluated at the unpenalised best response bounds the retained supremum.** False: the
maximisers readjust; the valid form is the dual Λ̂ (2.10a). At fixed sectors on the junta family the guaranteed relief
is 0.41 to 0.45 of the raw penalty, that is 7 to 14 % of the mixed-gain allowance (16 to 31 % for the raw penalty);
the retained element optimised together with the sectors removes 83 to 89 % of the K gain (11.15, 11.16). The two are
different quantities. **N**. `ch20`; `ch17`; `OCT/conjecture-K-report/conjecture_K_report.md`; KPR.

59\. **p ↦ log(E|X|^p/E|N|^p) is convex for chaos X** (it would give E|X| ≤ √(2/π)‖X‖₂). False on p ∈ (−0.8, 0.4) for
He₂, …, He₆. **N**. TAIL §5.

66\. **Disjoint redistribution 4.1 with a base H that depends on z₁.** False: H = −L·1{T ≥ 10}, U = sW gives a positive
linear difference for small s while satisfying the energy cutoff. **P, author only** (codex-a7c4) with exact scalar
support. A15 `CLAIMS.md`.

67\. **Conditional-Gaussian domination 5.2 with a conditioning field that depends on z₁.** False: 𝓕 = σ(z₁),
G = (a/2)W·1{T ≤ 1/100} concentrates variance where L is small. **P, author only**. A15 `CLAIMS.md`.

68\. **The remote cap Λ(q) ≤ S + η_R gives D(q) ≥ −η_R.** False as an inference: it gives
D(q) ≥ −[S − (B(u) − pu)] − η_R; budgets combine by 11.23. **P** (algebra). A15 `SECTORS.md`.

69\. **The total response energy is below 0.072.** False without a threshold in t: at the peak ε the bound 0.070564
needs t ≥ 0.8456 (use 0.080051 for t ≥ 0.84, 2.12b), and a genuine response at t = 0 has energy 0.273 (**N**; certified lower bound 0.0939738).
**P + C** (A15R and KPROG, reviewed). A15R `FOUNDATIONS.md` §2; KPROG `RESPONSE_ELLIPSOID.md`.

70\. **Over the zero core the odd sectors never raise Λ on the whole box, and the flank d-cost is 0.36.** False: at
(0.76, 0.76, 0.16), O = (i/100)Z(|Y|² − 1) gives Λ(O) − Λ(0) > 7·10⁻⁶; at the flank, O = (i/100)Z(T − 2) violates the
cost 0.36 by more than 5·10⁻⁷ (the valid value is 0.356, 5.4). **P + C** (5.4a).

71\. **Parity implies differentiability at every even L² base.** False: the even base H = L·sign(Re Z) has a positive
|τ| cusp along the genuine odd cubic Z(T − 2). **P** (A15R, reviewed). A15R `SECTORS.md` §6.

72\. **The formula of 7.4.1 holds for an indefinite cost matrix.** False: k_A = k_B = 1, m = 2, c = d = 0 give the value
0 while the gain is unbounded. **P**. A15R `SECTORS.md` §7.

73\. **Retaining the first-chaos correction restores full-box odd nonincrease.** False: the corner cubic of item 70
gives Λ̂(O) − Λ̂(0) > 7·10⁻⁶; the correction improves its value by less than 3.2·10⁻¹⁰. **P + C** (A15T, rev. #403).
The flank d-example of item 70 concerns Λ only: the retained flank d-cost exceeds 0.525048300689048 per unit cubic
energy (A15T `SECTORS.md` §3). A15T `SECTORS.md` §5.

74\. **Character exclusions alone give the weighted chaos bound of 6.3.9.** False outside the cubic chaos: for
independent standard complex X, Y, h_R = Y·1{|X| > R}/√P(|X| > R) has norm one and X-character zero, but
E[|X||h_R|²] > R. **P, author only** (A15T). A15T `CONTENT_KERNELS.md` §6.

77\. **The pointwise remainder of K over A, B, z₁Q, z̄₁P and d·z₁(T − 2) has a nonnegative cost.** False pointwise:
constant coefficients (k_Q, k_P, ν) = (i, 0, −i) have normalised local cost in
(−0.378784185484355, −0.378784185484354) < −3/8, and the pointwise Q/ν block has least eigenvalue −0.387101627…
(7.4.9). **P + C** (A15F, rev. #409) for the first number; the second has the status of 7.4.9 (A15V). Constant Q is not a centred quadratic: for genuine cubics the mixed Q/ν
term vanishes at the zero core because EQ = 0, so 5.4 stands; over a remote core the two channels (7.2.5) are absorbed by the
compensation of 7.2.10 (peak row), and over a core with A/B shells the interaction is open (7.2.4).

78, 3.5a. **SEI is preserved by adding any independent symmetric unimodal variable.** False: X₁ = Z₁Z₂Z₃/3 (genuine
real third chaos) is SEI, but X₁ + U with U uniform on [−1, 1] has SEI interval gap at least 91/18000 at radius 1/10.
Valid closures: independent centred Gaussian addition, or two SEI summands each with a symmetric unimodal density.
**P + C** (A15F; rev. #409, with a Monte Carlo gap 0.01131). No ledger result uses the false closure. A15F
`REFORMULATIONS.md` §§4–5; `reformulations_exact.py`; `ch32` ("Interval form (SEI) and sums").

80\. **The diffuse Gaussian attains the cubic supremum in each fixed dimension.** It is a limit over increasing
dimensions. At n = 1 the peak supremum is B₀ < S (2.6); the K_all equality in `ch32` needs a supremum over all
dimensions. **P** (A15F, rev. #409). A15F `REFORMULATIONS.md` §6.

81\. **The raw joint A/B form has a global cost 0.300.** False: the local cost at the peak is 0.298364… (7.4.9), and
along (1, −1/5) already 0.298526… (7.4.8). **P + C** (7.4.8; the value of 7.4.9 has the status of that row). The signed target (0.24, 0.26, 0.118) and K are unaffected.

84\. **A nonlinear function of a remote cubic is orthogonal to the first chaos because the cubic is.** False:
q = cX(|Y|² − 1) has Π₁ E_{z₁}ph(L + q) ≠ 0 (3.16). The diffuse benchmark of Λ̂ needs the explicit estimate 2.14.
**P + C** (3.16; A15S, rev. #419); A15S `FOUNDATIONS.md` §1.

85\. **The retained functional has the scaling law of the raw one.** False: the multiplier cost carries the weight λ
(3.17), and for q = cX(|Y|² − 1) the unweighted law fails strictly. A transfer must use k_λ and the energy range of
11.53. **P + C** (A15S, rev. #419). A15S `REMOTE.md` §§3–5.

86\. **The Q/ν mixed Hessian over a nonzero core has a single Hermitian radial kernel.** False: it has two channels,
and the genuine cubic directions (f, 1) and (if, i) at q = 0.001·X(|Y|² − 1) give coefficients below −8·10⁻⁶ and
above 4·10⁻⁶ (7.2.5). **P + C** (A15S, rev. #419). The zero-core cancellation and the costs 5.4 stand.
A15S `JOINT.md`.

87\. **A gain bound in terms of D₊ attenuates the remote error by 1 − κ.** False: the allowance (1 − κ)η_R + η belongs
to a gain bound in terms of the signed D; with D₊ the conclusion is S + η_R + η (11.23), as used in 11.51. **P + C**
(an exact scalar non-implication; A15S, rev. #419). A15S `REMOTE.md` §§1–2.

88\. **Shared-A domination holds for a residual with Gaussian law, orthogonal to the remote first chaos, with the
matched conditional covariance.** False: the witness of 6.5.1 gives Λ(G) > S + 7·10⁻⁷. It is not a cubic
(E‖DQ‖² > 3u₀), so it does not refute K; it shows that law-only arguments cannot prove K. **P + C** (A15S,
rev. #419). A15S `OBSTRUCTION.md`.

89\. **At the common remote trial, the odd increment over a joint core is paid by the U-allowance (O_U):**
J_odd(E, O) ≤ (1/100)‖r_B − 0.5053r_A‖² + 1/20000, where J_odd is the infimum over one global complex c of
\|c\|² + E[\|X + O − cz₁\| + \|X − O + cz₁\| − 2\|X\|] − p‖O‖² and X = L + E + (3/5)(r_A + 0.5053r_B). False: with one remote
coordinate Y, q = (57/5000)Y(\|Y\|² − 2), r_A = (133/1000)Y, r_B = (21√2/200)Y and
O = −i[(63/1000)z₁(\|Y\|² − 1) + (53/10000)z̄₁Y² + (7/5000)z₁(T − 2)], inside the energy and response caps, give
J_odd > 0.00021870 against an allowance below 0.000116077, an excess above 10⁻⁴ (the dual bound
J_odd ≥ A_dual − \|K_dual\|²/4 − 2E\|X\| − pW on 8,192,000 cells with Gaussian tails). Not a counterexample to the
all-sector target: the fixed-trial retained value at this witness is about 0.038 below S (numerical). The variant with remote re-optimisation on both sides is not refuted
(numerically 0.000112 against 0.000116 at this witness); 7.2.8 and 7.5.3 replace the statement. **P + C** (codex-91d4;
the dual bound re-derived and the countercertificate replayed by claude-b9ee). KODD `phase1/REPORT.md`,
`phase1/certification/PROOF.md`; `code/odd-over-AB-core-2026-11-15/`.

92\. **The terms of the deficit representations are separately nonnegative for every Gaussian-mixture profile.** False
for φ = √v. In 6.3.24, T₂ = E[Re(q̄²a)γ′(V)] is negative on q_θ = cos θ·X²Ȳ/√2 + sin θ·X(|X|² + |Y|² − 3)/√3 for
0 < θ ≤ 0.275: at θ = 0.2 and u = 1, T₂ = −1.32869·10⁻⁴ against D = 0.2455583 (the smallest value found on H₂,₁(ℂ²) is
−1.46·10⁻³·D), although D > 0 is proved for this cubic (3.25). The sign fails for small buffers only: the second
variation of T₂ at X²Ȳ/√2 changes sign near σ² = 0.027u, and at the peak row T₂/D ∈ [0, 0.414] and
T₁/D ∈ [0.586, 1] in every two-variable search. In 3.26 the comparison with the decoupled surrogate fails for √v:
E|q| = 0.6908 > E|q̃| = 0.6267 on the Wick monomial (exact). On the same cubic D − 𝒥 = −0.097·D at the buffer
σ² = 0.1u, and the second weighted term is −0.064·D at σ² = 0.3u. For exponential profiles both terms of 6.3.24 fail
(Wick monomial, λu = 30: T₁ = −0.0250; the cubic of item 5, λu = 3: T₂ = −0.0223). Three related monotonicity
statements are false as well: E[c₂ | V] nondecreasing; mean residual life of V at least u (it would give N ≥ 0 in
3.23, and N(300/u) = −0.108 for the cubic of item 5); and e^{ut²/4}·E J₀(t|q|) nondecreasing (Wick monomial:
E J₀(5|q|) = −0.0774). **N** (a research worker of claude-b9ee, three grids; the values of T₂ and D at θ = 0.1, 0.2,
0.25, 0.3 reproduced by claude-b9ee with its own formulas and quadrature); the two values on the Wick monomial are
exact. KDS `REPORT.md` §§3, 6.6, 7; `code/deficit-sign-2026-11-16/t10_T2_witness.py`,
`review-claude-b9ee/t2_witness_check.py`.

95\. **Gaussian Laplace domination and Gaussian small-ball domination on the real third chaos** (E e^{−tF²} ≥ (1 + 2t)^{−1/2}
for every t, or P(|F| ≤ s) ≥ P(|G| ≤ s) for every s, when E F² = 1; either would give E|F| ≤ √(2/π)). False in one
variable. For F = He₃(G)/√6 the density at zero is p_F(0) = √(2/3)·(1 + e^{−3/2})·φ(0) = 0.99868·φ(0), and
(p_F(0)/φ(0))² < 15606/15625 because e^{3/2} > 4585/1024. Explicitly, P(|G| ≤ 10⁻³) − P(|F| ≤ 10⁻³) > 827·φ(0)/750000000
and (1 + 2·10⁸)^{−1/2} − E e^{−10⁸F²} > 3·10⁻⁸; numerically the Laplace comparison fails for every t > 403.05
(by 1.7613·10⁻⁵ at t = 1000). It is not a counterexample to 3.6: E|F| = (1 + 4e^{−3/2})/√(3π) = 0.61646. Nor to the
smoothing sign of 3.21(d) and 3.22: on this F, C(s) < 0 for 0.1 ≤ s ≤ 10⁶; at large s the two contributions to the
Laplace difference are −0.2260 and +0.2274 in units of (2s)^{−1/2}, and their sum is 1 − p_F(0)/φ(0) = 0.00132. The
complex Wick monomial has no such obstruction, since |q|² has an unbounded density at 0; the complex counterexample is
item 5, in two variables. **P + C** (codex-6d92, #458: a rational certificate whose analytic steps are in its comments;
every step re-derived by claude-b9ee, the certificate replayed, and both gaps reproduced at 40 digits, 1.0517·10⁻⁶ and
9.3225·10⁻⁸); the threshold 403.05 and the values of C(s) **N** (claude-b9ee). TCH `codex-6d92/`;
`code/third-chaos-2026-11-16/codex-6d92/laplace_obstruction.py`, `review-claude-b9ee/`.

96\. **The holomorphic influence matrix of a remote cubic has no χ-control** (`../higher-degree` CONT5 §9.2, item 4, which has
the bound for the antiholomorphic matrix only). False: λ_max(J₁) ≤ u·(χ/2)^{1/2} (6.3.32 (c)); the example given for
the claim, X·P(Y) with χ = 2 and λ₁ = u, is the equality case in the limit. The conclusion of that item stands: the
bound is attained on a family of zero content, so it cannot make (F) see the phase (item 98). **P** (KIC, `ADDENDUM.md`
Corollary 13.1; re-derived by a review worker of claude-b9ee).

97\. **The bound of 6.3.31 is an equality on equal independent Wick blocks.** False from h = v³ on: for
q = :X²X̄: + :Y²Ȳ: and h = v³ the shadow A_h has the coefficient 36864 along :XȲ:, outside the range of 𝒟₁, and
(bound)²/‖c‖² = 1 + 1.009·10⁻⁴; at the peak profiles the ratio is 1.01–1.02 (**N**). It is an equality for h = v and
h = v². **C, author only** (exact; a review worker of claude-b9ee, correcting both versions of the delivery). KIC `REVIEW.md` §6 (F3).

11.6. **The weighted cubic contraction Re E[e^{−|q|²}·q̄²·Σ_j(∂\_jq)(∂_{j̄}q)] is nonnegative.** False:
q = X[5(|X|² − 2) + 6i(|Y|² − 1)]/√86 gives a value below −1/50 (interval certificate). **R** (replayed by its
recipient only). EXT-P; REV.

12.3. **The degree-5 analogue K₅ holds.** False numerically, at the flank rows and at the peak row. At t ∈ [0.86, 0.88]
one spike gives +1.55·10⁻⁵ (deterministic quadrature) and 8 to 20 spikes a plateau of about 8·10⁻⁵ ± 1·10⁻⁵; at the
peak row many spikes give +7.1·10⁻⁵ (`../higher-degree/STATUS.md` 4.9), where the families of
`sources/k5-2026-11-04/RESULTS.md` show no excess within their accuracy. **N**. The mechanism is **R**: a
sign-indefinite cross term in the conditional variance. The same term makes the cubic functional exceed S by more
than 1.5·10⁻⁴, at every row, on the affine model G = (σ\* − δ(T − 1))W′ with a suitable δ; that G lies in H₂,₁ ⊕ H₃,₂ and
is therefore no counterexample to K (LOG §11a). `sources/k5-2026-11-04/RESULTS.md`; MON §3.2; LOG §11a;
`../higher-degree/STATUS.md` 4.5, 4.9, 4.13.

102\. **The sign bias of a Hermitian quadratic form is at most (2/3)(Σλ³)².** For P = Σλ_j(T_j − 1), T_j independent
Exp(1), Σλ_j² = 1, the claim was α² = E[E(P|P²)²/P²] ≤ (2/3)(Σλ_j³)², a guess of the review of KCB. False for signed
spectra: λ = (3, 4, 5, −6)/√86 has Σλ³ = 0 and E[P⁵] = 24Σλ⁵ = −81216/86^{5/2} ≠ 0, while α² = 0 would give E[P|P²] = 0
almost surely and hence E[P⁵] = 0; by Cauchy–Schwarz α² ≥ E[P⁵]²/E[P¹⁰] = 318096/8940410521. So the bias is not of
the order (Σλ³)² for signed spectra; the proved bound α² ≤ min(1, 2√κ) of 6.3.36 stands. For spectra of one sign the
claim is open. **P** (codex-a7c4, in its review of pull request #480; the moments re-computed exactly by claude-b9ee).
KCB `REVIEW.md` §2.4 (the guess, kept verbatim).

104\. **ψ(u) ≥ −11/50 for every u > 0, for the characteristic function ψ(u) = E cos(u·He₃(X)/√6).** Announced at
the start of the Gemini proof of the diagonal case, 3.21 (e2), whose body argues the weaker ψ > −1/4, together with
|ψ| ≤ 3/8 on [12/5, ∞) and |ψ| < 1/√u, which have no valid proof there. All rest on the amplitude
e^{−1/2}(2/3)^{1/4} = 0.548 of a fold expansion, too small by the factor √2: the large-u amplitude is
(8/3)^{1/4}e^{−1/2} = 0.7751. False, even with −1/4: ψ(4727/1000) lies in −0.35433929299425 ± 4.73·10⁻¹⁵, and the
minimum of ψ is −0.354339, at u = 4.727. So that proof of the diagonal case is not a proof; 3.21 (e2) proves the case
by another route. **C**, author only (an Arb enclosure from the certificate of 3.21 (e2), replayed by claude-b9ee);
the minimum **N** (two quadratures that agree to 10⁻¹³). TCH2 `gemini-review/REVIEW.md` (B); TCH3a §7 (first row);
`code/third-chaos-2026-11-17/diag/psi_grid.txt` (the line k = 4727), `n01_psi_numerics.log`.

105\. **A cubic P of three real Gaussians with E|P| > (4/5)‖P‖₂.** Asserted, with no P given, in §1 of a text of
another assistant's session that the user pasted on 17 November 2026; since 4/5 > √(2/π), it would refute the d = 3
case of arXiv:2610.10477, Example 3.1. False: in three variables E|P| ≤ 0.72657‖P‖₂ (3.21 (e1), proved in §5 of
the same text, whose §4 retracts the claim), and in up to seven E|P| < 0.79717‖P‖₂. **P** (the n = 3 proof,
checked step by step by claude-b9ee). TCH2 `he3-radial-n3/REVIEW_claude-b9ee.md`, `PASTE2_radial_n3.md`.

106\. **Four claims of a Gemini answer on the weak target (T) of 3.21 (e9)** (17 November 2026; the answer is not kept,
and TCH3b records it). (a) Great-circle slicing proves the sharp bound for every F in at most 9 variables and (T) in

values are 0.80416, 0.81400, 0.82254, 0.83002 and 0.83664, so the plain slicing gives the sharp bound only for n ≤ 7

takes E(F² − 1)² = κ₄ instead of κ₄ + 2, and half of ∫₀^∞ s(1 + s²)^{−3/2}·min{1, αs²} ds = 2(√(α² + α) − α);
corrected, that argument gives the threshold 0.02684 (TCH3b). (d) Its printed "certified output" is not the output of its

107\. **Claims of the second and third Gemini answers on the weak target (T) of 3.21 (e9)** (17 November 2026; the
