## 14. Routes that do not work

What fails, and why. Each item is current knowledge; the sources have the details.

9\. **"Content ≤ f(x)" and "slack ≥ g(x)" through the fourth moment across all χ ∈ [0, 42] for raw K.** The ratio of the content term to the slack is 0.9 to 0.95 at the Wick monomial (**N**); scalar fourth-moment chains cover bounded kurtosis only (in `ch7` up to x = √χ ≤ 0.22 in floating point, **N**; with explicit O(δ^{3/2}) and OU-smoothed bounds, certified up to χ ≤ 0.2809 for the raw A-core in 6.3.40, **P + C**, and χ ≤ 841/10000 for the signed 10⁻⁶ deficit form in 6.1.5 and 6.3.43, **P + C, author only**).
`OCT/pipeline-kproof/PROOF_NOTES.md`; `ch7`; 6.3.40; KRAW.

12\. **The tangent step of Theorem A with a remote part.** A remote variance adds a positive tangent correction
(5.1a), so the step fails; this alone is not a counterexample to same-norm domination. **P** (audit, 5.1a).
LOG §10, §11e; ADD §2; A15 `CLAIMS.md`.

13\. **The conditional second moment vT + u(T − 1)² as a carrier of the A shell.** The profile (t − 1)² moves variance
to T ≈ 0, where |L| vanishes. **R**. LOG §11g; DIG §4.

14\. **Proofs from the law of the residual, from orthogonality, or from radial and Gaussian-noise tests alone.** The
Gaussian-marginal obstruction 6.5.1 (item 88), **P + C** there. `ch35`; LOG §11e, §11h.

15\. **Exact remote K from radial scalar inputs or moments alone.** Artificial radial laws beat the Gaussian payoff
(excess < 4.65·10⁻⁶); a moment-only relaxation with C₆ = 49.425 has a floor about 1.05·10⁻⁵ above S. The Stein
relaxation of 3.3, with C₆ = 40.45313, is not limited by it. **R**. DIG §3; `ch7`; A15 `REMOTE.md`.

16\. **Merging the Wick face and the Gaussian-factor face without chaos restrictions.** It fails for every normalised
fourth moment in [2.06, 6]. **R** ([P] in the report). `OCT/conjecture-K-report/conjecture_K_report.md`.

17\. **Inserting a buffer by Jensen, Λ(G) ≤ Λ(G + gU) + pg².** It costs p(9/40)² > 0.0669 against a budget of 9·10⁻⁵, so
buffered theorems do not transfer to zero buffer this way. **P** (arithmetic, with the buffer 9/40 of 5.5). DIG §3;
`lower-bound/obstruction-1402.tex`.

18\. **A pointwise tangent majorant for the A curvature at small buffer.** Its A-curvature is positive below
g ≈ 0.164: the tangent loses the radial part of the Hessian, about a factor 2. **R**. DIG §3; `ch55`.

21, 22, 6.5.2. **Leading-order (tensor) control and Taylor remainders for the content.** The optimal tensor constant
covers only u ≤ 0.0112, and the leading ratio overestimates the truth 9 times at u = 0.0463;
q₀ = X[−½(|X|² − 2) + Σ_{j≤4}(|Y_j|² − 1)] has zero leading tensor but E[X̄q₀|q₀|⁴] = −243; certified Taylor radii are
0.006 to 0.008 in amplitude against the needed 0.26, and cumulant-sensitive remainders reach u ≤ 3·10⁻⁵. The content
inequality is a finite-amplitude statement. **R**; the example q₀ **C** (audit, CERT §8). MON §3.4; LOG §12b, §12c, §12e;
CERT §8.

23\. **A global sixth-derivative bound for the deficit.** Negative beyond u = 0.0017 (6.3.11). **R**. LOG §12f; DIF §4.

24\. **The quadratic shell cost absorbing the content without the remote slack.** It needs w > 0.038 at u = 0.0463.
**R**. LOG §11i.

25, 6.1.3. **Fourth-moment envelopes in (u, w).** The refinements of 6.1.2 gain little: tighter enclosures move the cutoff
from 0.01125 to 0.011223, and the envelope with fourth-moment data is larger than the universal one at
(u, w) = (0.0463, 0.01125): 0.0021574 against 0.0021472 above B₀. These cutoffs use the benchmark gap
S − B₀ > 0.0020476 of `ch61`. With the gap 0.002074245 of section 0 both envelopes pass that corner, and the bound of
6.1.2 as refined in LOG §12d gives the cutoff w ≥ 0.01111 (arithmetic on that bound, in the records audit). **R**.
LOG §12d, §12j; MON §3.4.

29\. **A purely radial polynomial envelope with the two radial moment constraints at the pure-P end.** A three-point law
gives more than 0.246 (certified obstruction, **R**); the affine weight of 6.3.3 avoids it. LOG §23;
`RCV/r26_envelope_audit/`.

30\. **The directional envelope on the small-Wick strip of 6.3.14 (sampled 0.2393).** An eight-atom witness forces its cap above
0.2405 (**R**: reported by a thread whose archive does not exist). LOG §29.

31\. **Hessian perturbation from the Wick endpoint beyond E|P|² ≤ u/5 (6.3.6).** It loses a factor of about 20 on the
content. **R**. LOG §19, §24.

32\. **Adding family certificates up to a general theorem; "several drivers follow driver by driver".** The functional
is not additive, the families do not exhaust H₂,₁, and conditioning on one driver leaves the others inside g(|q|²).
**P** (second audit). REC §4; CERT (conclusion).

33\. **"Every mechanism that spreads q lowers the content faster than the deficit."** Supported on some families, not
a theorem: **open**. DIF §4.

35\. **An auxiliary diffuse Gaussian W used as a direction.** √u·W is not in H₂,₁ and not an admissible first-chaos
multiplier; counterexamples built on it are spurious. **P** (second audit). DIF §3; A15V `FOUNDATIONS.md`.

40, 7.3.3. **Integrating the infinitesimal signed A/B bound 7.3.2 along q + s(A + B).** The intermediate bases depend
on z₁ and are not remote. **P** (audit). ASM §3.3.

41, 7.3.4. **The pointwise finite-amplitude Hessian form at intermediate bases.** Its smallest eigenvalue is −0.03 at
|β| = 0.1 and −0.84 at |β| = 0.3 (**N**, peak, grid). LOG §41; `code/experiments-2026-11-04/e11_ab_kernel_finite.py`.

42\. **Closing A + B through the near-exact mixed coefficient m = 0.118.** Fragile: with the alignment 7.1.4 and Δ ≤ 1.6u
the excess is 5.5·10⁻⁵ at m = 0.118, 1.2·10⁻⁴ at 0.115 and 8·10⁻⁴ at 0.10 (**R**; the three values recomputed from the
formula of LOG §44 in the records audit). With the centred alignment 7.1.6 it is below 1.9372·10⁻⁵ at m = 0.118
(7.4.2); the values at smaller m are not recomputed for it. LOG §44, §45.

51\. **K_rot alone (2.11).** It pays only 13 to 57 % of the measured mixed gain (a subtraction at fixed sectors), and no
cap is proved for it. **R**. DIG §2; `ch23`.

52\. **Scalar Rice cuts and moments on the response side.** Saturated: they improve the peak cap by less than
4.3·10⁻⁷, and a law with nodes 2 ± √2 passes every scalar cut. **R** ([P, C] in the manuscript). DIG §1 item 4.

53\. **Proportional remote tests v = λq in the response-body dual.** They leave a gap of 0.0007977 at e = 1/25. **R**.
DIG §2.

54\. **Concentration tests as a route to small influence.** They do not force it (the family
Q_N = X₁(Σ_j|X_j|² − N − 1)/√(N + 1)). **R**. DIG §3.

55\. **A better certificate at ε = 149/200, or rearranged gates.** At that ε no certificate reaches
1.402 (8.3); the rearranged gate scheme has a worse Gaussian ideal (0.713519).
**R**. `OCT/pipeline-gate/KSIDE.md`; MON §1.

56\. **The frozen table plus a cut-bank relaxation.** Four atomic laws pass all 671 cuts with values above 500/701; the
genuine-cubic floor is 0.71335 at ε = 149/200. **R** ([P, C] in the manuscript). DIG §1 items 22, 23.

57\. **A degree-5 witness with gates or independent Gaussian tests.** Gated rows lose at least 5.5·10⁻³ on the k-side
and up to 2·10⁻² with the h-side shells (LOG §2; `../higher-degree/STATUS.md` 4.6 has 3–4·10⁻³ from KSIDE §6), and
exact K₅ fails (12.3). The gate-free programme with an allowance for K₅ is `../higher-degree/STATUS.md` §4. **R**.
LOG §2; `sources/k5-2026-11-04/RESULTS.md`.

58\. **Second-order tests at the diffuse contact.** An exact flat sector: they cannot separate the diffuse contact.
**R**. LOG §12a.

60\. **Reduction to the sphere S³ ⊂ ℂ².** Valid but useless: every S³ instance has ratio ≤ K_G^ℂ(2) ≈ 1.2. TAIL §5.

61\. **A shift by the vector-valued Borell inequality.** The Borell deficit of a driver pair is about 10⁻² against a
budget of 10⁻⁴ (hand estimate). TAIL §5.

62\. **A Bellman function or martingale relaxation in (t, f_t, g_t).** It cannot see the sign of the time multiplier
(an argument, not computed). TAIL §5.

63\. **Sums of squares of degree 2.** They give only the trivial value 1 for sup ‖P₁,₀f‖² (the truth is π/4); degree 4
is untested. TAIL §5.

64\. **Reading a finite-dimensional scan as a bound.** See the label **N** (section 0) and the reading of the REP rows in
section 9. REP §5.

65\. **The external "proofs" of K\*, of K and of an unconditional 1.4022** (LOG §9, §11c, §11f, §12h) are wrong; the
point-by-point refutations are there (for K and 1.4022: a false Schur-convexity and dropped shell cross terms, items 26
and 27). **R**.

75, 11.18. **Bounding the tilted content without the structure of the deficit.** Cauchy–Schwarz through the law of
|q|² alone, ‖Π₁[q·h_s(|q|²)]‖² ≤ min_κ E[|q|²(h_s(|q|²) − κ)²], does not vanish at a Gaussian law: for the exponential
law of |q|² at u = 0.0463 the bound over the cost is 9.9·10⁻⁴ for K and 1.6·10⁻⁴ at the best tilt, against the slack
1.31·10⁻⁴ of 1.402. The structural bound 11.29 with its constant weight is 3 to 10 times too weak on [0.02, 0.0463] on
the Wick monomial, where D/δ falls from 0.73 to 0.13. What works is 11.49 with 11.50: energy-dependent coefficients
γ(u)·δ against a deficit certified in (u, χ). **N** (one tensor for the second part). KPR §10; TILT-REV, TILT-REV2;
`code/record-checks-2026-11-15/tilted_content_check.py`.

76\. **The tilted sector added to the headline remote cap as a black box.** With ‖c‖² ≤ 1/2500 and 11.40, the cap 3.3
gives only Λ(q) + sup_r 𝒥(q, r) < S + 0.001283, 64 times the allowance 2·10⁻⁵: the sector term must enter the
certificate (11.44) or be compared with D (11.50). **P** (arithmetic). TILT-5 §4; `secant_assembly.md` §4.

79\. **Real BNC as the real form of PVD.** Only BNC ⇒ real PVD ⇒ complex PVD is proved, with no converse (3.5, **P** there). A15F
`REFORMULATIONS.md` §§1–3.

83\. **The October moment-polynomial bank alone, for the augmented target 11.44 everywhere.** At (u, χ) = (1/25, 4)
every one of its 154 polynomials, with either reviewed content surcharge, exceeds S_upper + 0.0002 (exact); Stein cuts
or a sharper content budget are needed there, as in 11.50. **C** (exact finite-bank obstruction, A15V `REMOTE.md` §5;
accepted as valid in the review of #413 (F3) with the checker replayed). A limit of that bank of upper bounds only, not
a lower bound on Λ̂.

90\. **Sharpening only the universal scalar Stein budget.** The Clifford cubic of 11.60 has ℬ(q) = (109/19)δ, against the
universal ℬ ≤ 6δ. So the best universal coefficient lies in [109/19, 6], and improving it gains at most a factor
114/109 < 1.046 on any bound linear in the budget, not the 1.5–3 that the content bounds need. (The first witness,
q = Z₀²·conj(Z₁) with ℬ = 200 = 5δ, gave 6/5.) Both cubics have zero content (11.31a): the information lies in the
projection, not in the budget. **P + C** (codex-a786; re-derived, and the moments recomputed by a second exact method,
by claude-b9ee). KMA `PROOFS.md` §3; KPH `PROOFS.md` §7.

91\. **Cauchy–Schwarz on the antiholomorphic Stein pair, for the raw content inequality.** The bound 6.3.25 reaches
δΩ_e = 0.2293·D on H₂,₁(ℂ²) at u = 0.0463 for the raw profile (section 9, the KAS row). So it cannot give the constants
0.10 or 0.15 of 7.4.2; it does not exclude 0.24 (6.2.1, 6.2.3), with a margin of 4.5 % in two variables. With the true
norms of the four components Cauchy–Schwarz still gives 0.173·D at the maximiser: a proof of the raw inequality along
this route has to use the signs and angles of the four pairings, not only their norms. **N** (lower bounds on suprema;
two variables). KAS `README.md` (O1); `REVIEW.md` §5. The class results 6.3.27–6.3.30 do not resolve this item: they
change the representative before Cauchy–Schwarz (a conditional mean given the modulus of the cofactor, or a polynomial
with zero conditional mean given q), on classes without self-interaction, and give no better majorant of the same four
norms.

93\. **Cauchy–Schwarz on the local pair (a, DF·F), for the raw content inequality.** The representation 6.3.26 has a
smaller budget than 6.3.25 and no mixture field, and its two pairings add constructively (0.3–0.6 and 0.4–0.8 of the
content); but its supremum over H₂,₁(ℂ²) is above the constants 0.10 and 0.15 of 7.4.2 (6.3.26 has the values; the
largest was found from two starts, at χ = 20.4). As for item 91, a proof has to use the angles between the fields,
not only their norms. **N** (lower bounds on suprema; two variables; author only). KDS `REPORT.md` §6.5, F12.

94\. **Conditioning on one coordinate, beyond the cubics that vanish on a hyperplane.** The tangent and Jensen bounds
of 3.25 use only E[|q|² | X]. The component of q free of X raises that conditional variance at X = 0 and enters with a
negative coefficient, so the criterion fails for a third of random three-variable cubics and for every one of a
hundred random four-variable cubics (for N equal Wick monomials and the symmetric direction that component carries
(N − 1)(N − 2)/N² of the energy). On the class itself neither bound gives the smoothing sign of 3.22: the first-order
coefficient near the conditionally Gaussian corner is +π/8, of the wrong sign, unless ‖ℓ‖ ≥ |α|; the sign there
depends on the fluctuation of |Q(Y)|², not on its mean. **N** for the counts; **P, author only** (the worker alone) for
the smoothing remark. KDS `REPORT.md` §§5.4, 5.6, F9, F10.

98\. **Sharper influence bounds in (F), including Gram dominance.** For 10 ≤ χ ≤ 42 the corner (2u, u) is attained
(6.3.33), so no constraint on the influences in terms of (u, χ) lowers the bound (F) of 6.3.34; for χ < 10 the gain is
the cap set of 6.3.32 (c), at most ×2.2 at χ = 2. The binding cells of the degree-5 band are at χ = 16 and 30
(`../higher-degree/STATUS.md` 4.25), so no row closes through the influences. Most of the loss of 6.3.31 on the corner
family is the misalignment of the shadows with the influence directions (×7 at χ = 16), not the size of the
influences: at χ = 10 the family reaches X²Ȳ, whose content vanishes while every factor of the bound stays of order
one. Not excluded: constraints that couple the influences to the law of |q|. **P** for the corner, **N** for the
numbers. KIC `ADDENDUM.md` §§3–5; `REVIEW.md` §8.

99\. **Selecting or conditioning on boundedly many original coordinates.** For q_m = X·Σ_{j≤m}(|Y_j|² − 1)/√m and a
k-dimensional subspace E of the span of the original coordinates, ‖E[q_m | σ(E)]‖² = (c/m)(k − 1 + 2(1 − c)²) with
c = ‖Proj_E X‖²: at most 8/(27m) for k = 1 and (k − 1)/m for 2 ≤ k ≤ m + 1, although X has full influence; (X, q_m)
converges in law to (X, X·G) with G a real Gaussian. So no function of boundedly many original coordinates captures
the cubic in L². Not excluded: reductions on new coordinates, approximation in law, trace corrections. **P**
(codex-a786; re-derived by a review worker of claude-b9ee, with a second proof) + **C** (66 random rational subspaces).
KCB `COORDINATE_OBSTRUCTION.md`; `REVIEW.md` §6.

100\. **A finite-dimensional certificate for the content law through random compression.** A variance-one cubic in any
dimension has a variance-one compression in m + 1 variables (m ≥ 842) with |Δχ| ≤ 44000/√m and the content, energy
and law functionals of a fixed normalised profile changed by at most (3B + 37L)/√m (B, L its sup and Lipschitz
norms). The errors are absolute, of order ‖Q‖₈⁴/√m for the fourth excess (2755/√m on the junta), while the margin of
the inequality is of order θ·χ·Ω̂. The resulting test cannot hold for m ≤ 1,097,505 for any profile; on the six band
rows of `../higher-degree/STATUS.md` 4.19 the junta needs m ≥ 1.7·10⁷ to 2.1·10⁹ (**N**), where the coefficient
space has more than 10²⁰ dimensions. The compression itself is correct and gives the rate of 3.28. **P** (a GPT session
of the user; re-derived by a review worker of claude-b9ee); the row constants **N**. KFR `REDUCTION.md` §§1–4;
`REVIEW.md` §§2–3.

101\. **Tangent controls instead of the averaging of sign branches.** For q = X·P_m (6.3.28) and the representative
Z = X(2|X|²C − 1) of 6.3.23, every N in the L² closure of tangent divergences (vector fields v with D_vq = D_vq̄ = 0)
has (2/(3δ))(‖Z + N‖² + |⟨Z + N, q⟩|²) ≥ (32/m + 9μ_m²/(1 − β_m²))/(6 + 18/m) ≥ 6/e² > 4/5, with μ_m = E|P_m| and
β_m = E[P_m|P_m|], because q·sign P is constant along such fields. So the thresholds 0.37 and 0.64 of the first two
band rows cannot be reached on these cubics by tangent controls and the law-norm Cauchy–Schwarz alone, for any m. The
bound is 1.594 at m = 1 and tends to 3/π. A sharper floor is θ(E[Z | q, sign P]) = 1.639, 1.354, 1.307, 1.085 at
m = 1, 4, 5, 21 (**N**, the review): the tangent divergence of `../higher-degree/STATUS.md` 4.21 is within 2.6 % of it.
The constants of 6.3.28 and of 4.22 there, which tend to 0 with m, use the reflection (X, P) → (−X, −P), which is not a
tangent control. Not excluded: profile-specific estimates, non-local operations, other cubics (for the blocks of
6.3.39 the conditional variance does not vanish and no sign averaging is needed). **P** (codex-a786; re-derived by a
review worker of claude-b9ee, who adds the sharper floor); the values **N**. KST `TANGENT_OBSTRUCTION.md`; `REVIEW.md`
§§2.6, 6.

103\. **The coefficient 3/20 in 11.61 from the 119 duals of 11.50.** At (u, χ) = (463/10000, 3) the smallest certified
content envelope is 0.000209009775 (the coefficient 13/400) and the best of the nine duals at that energy gives the
deficit lower bound 0.000845094962, so this pair needs a coefficient above 0.2402 at the allowance 6·10⁻⁶ (or an
allowance above 8.2·10⁻⁵ at 3/20); at (11/400, 1229/100), with eight duals, above 0.2505 with the coefficient
393/10000 of 11.62 (0.2747 with 1859/43200). A limit of this bank of upper bounds, as in item 83, not a lower bound on
the true coefficient: on 33 finite cubics the largest ‖c_{3/5}‖²/D is 0.0127 (**N**, author). The sampled richer duals
of the delivery raise the two deficit bounds by 3 % and 8 % only (**N**, author). **C** (exact arithmetic on the saved
bank: codex-91d4; replayed, and both numbers recomputed, in the review of KAB; the value 0.2505 is the review's). KAB
`BUDGET_AUDIT.md` §3, `route2/DIAGNOSTICS.md`, `CONTENT_NUMERICS.md`; `content_structure_exact.py`.

## 15. Open problems, in priority order

A bound sup Λ̂ ≤ S + η on the rows of a consumer gives the lower bound (2.10a); K itself is not needed for it. Part A is
ordered by what a proof of 1.402 through Λ̂ needs first; part B lists what K needs in addition.

**A. The bound through the retained functional Λ̂**

1. **Completed.** The joint remainder premise (P) of 7.4.10 holds for every (w, A, B) ∈ ℂ³ at the peak row (7.4.16, **P + C**). The bridge of 7.4.10 therefore gives Λ̂(q + A + B) < S + 4936/10⁸ (**P + C**) and `< S + 29.36·10⁻⁶` (`D_+` 11.61) / `< S + 27.00·10⁻⁶` (signed 11.61, strictly `< S` on $u \in [0, 0.0354] \cup [0.0456, 0.0463]$; **P + C**, `KAST`) for u ≤ 0.0463 and u + V ≤ 0.080051 with arbitrary sharing and phases (plus `< S + 1.078·10⁻⁴` / `< S + 1.070·10⁻⁴` for u ≤ λ²·0.0463, u + V ≤ λ²·0.080051 across the 11.53 profile rectangle). A second target,
   with no caps on the driver energies but with content premises that are open, is (J) of 7.4.15.
2. **The odd sectors over cores with A/B shells**: the averaged premise (P_Ψ) of 7.5.3, or the compact averaged bound
   of its centred joint form (7.5.6). **Independently reviewed:** remote cores (7.2.10), q = 0 (7.5.1), every admissible core with W ≤ 1/1800000 and on the wider harmonic-dependent strips (7.5.4c), and the regions 7.5.7, 7.5.8, 7.5.10,
   7.5.11, and 7.5.12 (\|q\| ≥ 11/8 pointwise, unconditional cone W ≥ 179V, and unconditional E F_joint(c\*) < 50·10⁻⁶ for all V ≤ 11/100000 without assuming (P)), plus the affine W-region of 7.4.16 (including W ≥ 150V); under (P_Ψ) alone, `KAST` certifies the pure-`D_+` cession `J_AB + 0.01‖U‖² − D₊ < 29.71·10⁻⁶` (`< S + 79.71·10⁻⁶` total). **Independently reviewed peak-row proof and replay.** row 7.5.13 certifies $F_{\mathrm{joint}}(c^*) \le 0$ pointwise on all of $\mathbb{C}^6$ at the peak row (no $|\nu|^2 \le 1/50$ cap), giving unconditional six-sector $\widehat{\Lambda}(G) < S + 29.36\cdot 10^{-6}$ / $< S + 27.00\cdot 10^{-6}$ (strict $< S$ on $u \in [0, 0.0354] \cup [0.0456, 0.0463]$; `KAST`, from $4936/10^8$ in `KGAS`) throughout $u \le 0.0463, u + V \le 0.080051$ for all $W \ge 0$ and arbitrary sharing. Excluded routes: a pure increment bound (7.2.9), the common-trial allowance (item 89), cutoffs with positive
   moment charges (7.5.5), perturbation around the shell-free case (7.5.8).
3. **A consumer whose rows these bounds fit** (section 8). The single peak row covers t ∈ [0.84, 0.851] at η = 10⁻⁴ (8.1), and 6 subdivided peak-row $t$-slabs use 7.5.13 to widen this to **`[0.8400, 0.8661]`** (`KAST`, closing the upper gap `[0.851, 0.863]` of 8.7 at ε = 1891/2500). Known
   covers: one new row at ε = 149/200, which gives 1.40171 (below the proved lower bound, so a test of K only) and needs its own remote cap, (P) and odd premise, because
   the transfer from the peak loses too much (8.5, 8.6); ten central rows at ε = 299/400 for 1.402, whose binding rows
   admit 3.0 to 4.4·10⁻⁵ (8.9, 8.10; subdivided scaled-peak slabs in `KAST` cover `[0.84000, 0.84948]` by 7.5.13); nine central rows on [0.795, 0.864] at ε = 3/4
   (exterior from Lean's `Lower.ρ` alone, allowances > 7.57·10⁻⁵; `[0.84000, 0.85168]` across Rows 4–6 closed using 7.5.13 in `KAST`) and thirteen central rows on [0.7515, 0.863] at
   ε = 94/125 (exterior using ρ = 0.93462, allowances > 1.021·10⁻⁴ > 10⁻⁴ on every row, with Row 8 `[0.840, 0.847]` directly closed `< 6.35·10⁻⁵` / `< 6.11·10⁻⁵ < 10⁻⁴` and subdivided scaled-peak slabs closing `[0.8400, 0.8547]` across Rows 8–10 using 7.5.13 in `KAST`; the exterior and row-bank benchmarks retain their 8.12, 8.13 author-only grades); three remaining lower response intervals at ε = 1891/2500
   (8.7, 8.11). Also open: the remote and retained all-sector caps on the central rows outside $t \ge 0.84$ (`u_0 > 0.0463`) and outside the peak tube/rectangle
   (3.11, 3.15 and 11.53; the claims 11.7 to 11.11 are unchecked), the corner σ\* = 0 if a cover uses it (2.5), and a
   Lean statement of the consumer.
4. **Numerics for Λ̂ with more than three remote variables** (11.20).

**B. Conjecture K itself**

5. **The finite-amplitude joint A/B bound** (7.4): **certified globally on all of $\mathbb{C}^3$ at the three rows** in 7.4.17 — proving both the signed and robust forms of 7.4 / 7.4.5 with reserve $1/100 \gg 8.07\cdot 10^{-5}$ and the global common diagonal costs $\kappa_{\mathrm{diag}} = 7/25 = 0.28$ (`peak`), $3/10 = 0.30$ (`flank`), $31/100 = 0.31$ (`upper`), strictly below the local obstructions of 7.4.9, with no shell-energy cap (and arbitrary remote $L^2$ drivers in the remainder/cost bounds).
6. **The raw content inequality** on 0.00551 ≤ u ≤ 0.0463 (6.3.18), with the constant set by item 5 for first-chaos drivers (7.4.2: $11/50 = 0.22$ under Target D of 7.4.17, or $6/25 = 0.24$ under signed Target S; 0.10 without 7.4.17); the
   B-content then follows by the alignment 7.1.4 and 7.1.6 (7.1.5). Under 7.4.17, strong $C^2 \le \gamma D$ covers 6.3.3, 6.3.4, 6.3.41, the complete one-driver self-interaction family across all shares and phases (6.3.5, 6.3.13–6.3.16, author only), pure and bounded-ratio shared-bath multi-driver self-interactions (6.3.10, 6.3.17, author only, with $9/100 < 1/10$ on pure $q_0$), and the exact zero-slack domain $\chi \le 49/625$ of 6.3.43(c) (author only); carrying $(25/6)\eta_c$ explicitly covers the signed $+10^{-6}$ regime $\chi \le 841/10000$ of 6.3.43(c) ($< S + 6.90\cdot 10^{-6}$, author only); and combining Target S with 3.3 (`gain/allowance < 1`) covers the weak-allowance regions of 6.3.40 (staircase and $\tau_{\max} \le 0.054$) and 6.3.42 ($\ge 95$ equal blocks) to $< S + 10^{-4}$. Row 6.3.44 gives the signed strong bound on a wider staircase and on diffuse disjoint blocks. Open pieces at intermediate/high χ: comparable-energy even driver shells beyond 6.3.9c/6.3.16b and arbitrary O(1) bath shares on many self-interacting drivers beyond 6.3.17(b). What the
   representations give and cannot give: 6.3.23 to 6.3.39 with items 91, 93 and 98; zero-content classes: 11.31a, 11.56.
7. **Exact remote K** (3.6), not needed at the peak (3.3): known on the classes named in 3.6 and proved, by their
   author only, on those of 3.24 and 3.25; routes 3.20, 3.21 (d), 3.26.
8. **The sharp radius ρ\* = √π/2 on H₂,₁** (3.6; equivalent to PVD, 3.5, and implied by the sharp real third-chaos bound, 3.21 (e)) and the real weak target E\|h\| ≤ √0.68‖h‖₂ (3.21 (a), (e9)). Directly on H₂,₁, ρ\* ≤ 2287/2500 = 0.9148 < 0.916 is proved and certified outside Lean (3.7, **P + C**; 4571/5000 = 0.9142 is **P, author only + C**), passing the radius condition of the uncapped degree-5 game.

**C. Other routes to the same decimals** have their own ledgers. The identity-tail witness, designed for a Lean bound above
1.4018 (section 10; [`../identity-tail/STATUS.md`](../identity-tail/STATUS.md)): its integer
certificate, with a margin of 5.6·10⁻⁵ to 5000/7009, is in Lean since #525 and gives, after antiholomorphic Stein tightening of its two binding rows and the 93/100 cubic radius, the proved lower bound K_G^ℂ > 1.402 (`main_bounds`). The games of degree 5 and higher ([`../higher-degree/STATUS.md`](../higher-degree/STATUS.md)): the open steps
are listed in section 5 of that ledger; the one that uses rows of this ledger (6.3.23 to 6.3.39 and 11.31a) is a
content lemma on a band of energies, on the rows t ∈ [0.80, 0.855], open in both directions (4.28 there).
